# Models remediation plan, rebased on the accepted decision records

**Status:** proposed, not started. Nothing here has been built or run. Every slice was verified on
2026-11-18 against `40c9036fdc`.

**Replaces:** the 7-slice "finstack-quant-models & Bindings Blocker/Major Remediation" plan for the
23 findings of the models stepwise quant audit. That plan patched the current API shapes; this one
implements what the accepted records under `docs/decisions/financial/` prescribe for the same code.

**How it was produced:** each slice was drafted from a full read of the "Accepted decision" section
of every governing record plus the source the finding touches (Slices 1a to 7 on 2026-11-16,
Slice 0 on 2026-11-18). On 2026-11-18 each slice was then verified independently against
`40c9036fdc`: every defect line re-read in current source, every cited decision re-read in its
record, every count re-derived by grep, and the cross-slice hand-offs written into the receiving
slice. Across roughly 560 decision citations the verification changed about 20; counts and file
lists changed far more often. Each slice carries a `**Verified:**` line that names what was not
checked. Two limits apply to all of it: nothing was compiled or run, and both passes were done
by AI agents reading source, so treat a cited decision number as a pointer to read, not as a
substitute for the record.

## Scope rule

For each finding, a slice implements:

1. the record decisions that govern the finding's code path, and
2. any decision that deletes, renames or re-signatures an API the fix touches, so nothing built in
   the slice is removed later (`AGENTS.md`: no stopgaps, no compatibility layers).

Every other decision in the record is listed under **Deferred** in the slice with the reason it is
separable. Finding numbers (#1 to #23) are the audit's.

## What changed from the old plan

The old plan's "Confirmed Design Decisions" 2, 3 and 4 are withdrawn; the records contradict them.
Decision 1 ("no signature overhauls") is withdrawn because most records are signature overhauls.

## Size

Slices 1a to 7 change or remove roughly 240 public Rust items (1a ≈ 16, 1b ≈ 17, 2 ≈ 61, 3 ≈ 38,
4 ≈ 44, 5 ≈ 32, 6 ≈ 27, 7 ≈ 8), most mirrored in Python and WASM. The owner accepted the scope
rule when the estimate was about 210; verification raised Slices 2, 3 and 4. These are counts by
enumeration from the remedy steps, not compiler output.

Slice 0 adds roughly 29 more public items and about 510 call sites (time grids 203, Philox 234,
PCG 61, quadrature 14 production). Three of its steps move pinned numbers: 0a (uniform knot bits;
near-coincident events become real steps), 0c-3 (every PCG sequence and Philox normal, once) and
0b (last bits of every quadrature consumer).

This is a reconciliation programme, not a patch set.

## Proposed order

Ordering follows what each record delegates to. It is a proposal; the open decisions below can move it.

0. **Slice 0, core prerequisites** (drafted below): quadrature, time grids, the PCG and Philox
   generators, and the domain-neutral `HestonParams` reshaping. It can run alongside steps 1, 2
   and 4, which do not depend on it. Levenberg–Marquardt and Brent have already landed.
1. **Slice 3, forward/spot Greeks and lookback (#14, #15).** MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 is the forward law
   that 1a, 1b and 2 price with.
2. **Slice 1a**, then **Slice 1b**.
3. **Slice 3, COS and Heston (#13).**
4. **Slice 2** in four sub-slices: formula and single entry point; calibration; smile repair; cube.
5. **Slice 6**, **Slice 5**, **Slice 4**, once their Slice 0 steps are in: Slice 6 needs 0a
   (both its grids), 0c (PCG for the toggle, Philox for portfolio loss) and 0b-a; Slice 5 needs
   0a and, for its Heston steps 15-17, 0d and Slice 3 #13b; Slice 4 needs 0a and 0c-1/0c-2.
6. **Slice 7** last; it collects what the others leave.

## Decisions needed before starting

1. **Scope.** The owner accepted the scope rule on 2026-11-18, when the plan was Slices 1a to 7
   (about 210 public items). Slice 0 was added afterwards and verification raised the total to
   about 240 plus Slice 0; neither is covered by that acceptance.
2. **Blocker #3.** Close as not reproducible and fold into #4, or keep as its own item.
3. **Blocker #7.** The record keeps flat barriers, so a healthy firm with C = P and r > 0 still
   elects PIK. Close as "model as specified, inputs explicit", or have the bond side derive a
   distinct PIK amount P in the same slice.
4. **Core prerequisites.** DECIDED 2026-11-18: this plan owns Slice 0. Other remediation plans
   that stand on the same core records should depend on it rather than land their own version. Its own open
   questions remain, chiefly the `max_evals` values for existing quadrature callers, accepting
   ulp-wide steps in production grids, and the stream value for seed-only PCG callers.
5. **Unowned credit wire types.** CREDIT-01, -02, -06 and -20 reshape the scoring, master-scale,
   rating-factor, workout/waterfall and LME types and no slice owns them. Add a slice, or close that
   part of #23 against the credit follow-ups.
6. **SABR references.** MODELS-ANALYTIC-13 d24 rules out the existing Hagan/QuantLib goldens for
   off-ATM quotes. Someone has to supply independently derived references.
7. **Splits.** Slice 2 into four, Slice 3 into three, and whether rough Heston (record 12) and the
   uncovered FX decisions (record 17) get their own slices.

8. **Record 09 with the Heston work.** Take MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d2-22 and d24 into Slice 3 #13b
   (removes `heston_call_price_fourier`, 8 caller files), or keep it out and accept that the strip
   edits and the `v0 = 0` cutoff seed are built and later replaced.
9. **Severity of #13b.** The Heston false overflow is reachable only at very small vol-of-vol
   with `kappa < rho * sigma_v`, or on a dense custom grid; default settings do not hit it. Keep
   as Major or re-rank.
10. **Exact dividend placement (Slice 5 #5).** 12 of the 36 QuantLib Leisen–Reimer oracle cases
    would be rejected, not shifted, because their Bermudan dates are not tree nodes. Accept, or
    select the grid from the dates.

Each slice ends with its own narrower open questions.

The table above was a signature-level check and is superseded by Slice 0 where they differ:
quadrature also lacks the explicit work limit (d4); time grids also lack d3-6 and d10; Philox
also lacks d2 and d4; the Heston domain change itself is in Slice 3 #13b, with only the
domain-neutral reshaping (0d) in Slice 0; Slice 1b does not depend on the Heston domain; and
Slice 6 does need time grids (CREDIT-05 d8 and CREDIT-11 d6 name the canonical uniform grid).

Consequence for ordering: Slice 3 (#14, #15), Slice 1a, the ATM and SVI parts of 1b and Slice 2
can start now. Variance replication (1b) waits on 0b-a and Slice 3 #14; Slices 4, 5 and 6 wait
on 0a; Slices 4 and 6 wait on 0c; the Heston parts of Slices 3 and 5 wait on 0d.

### Work with no owning slice

The hand-offs between slices are now written into the receiving slices. What remains unowned:

- **Credit wire types** reshaped by CREDIT-01 (18 public Rust items), CREDIT-02 (27), CREDIT-06
  (82) and CREDIT-20 (10). Slice 7 only adds unknown-field rejection for them. See decision 5.
- **MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 and -09** (CIR and classical Heston QE): the classical Monte Carlo
  admission gate and its `1e-10` cutoff stay until an owner takes them.
- **CORE-NUMERICS-17 d8, d10, d11** (Brownian-bridge numerics; Slice 4 takes d7 only) and
  **d12-15** (Poisson quantile).
- **MODELS-ANALYTIC-03** (implied-volatility inverse): Slice 2's smile repair would be written
  against today's inverse.
- **MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 remainder** (Heston Fourier integration): not cleanly separable from
  Slice 3 #13b; held out on size. See decision 8.
- **MODELS-ANALYTIC-17 remainder** (FX delta smiles, d4-8 and later): no audit finding covers it.
- **`map_date_to_step`** in the convertible valuator (CORE-NUMERICS-20 d12) and the three
  `dt(0)` caches in `discretization/{qe_heston,exact_hw1f,schwartz_smith}.rs` (d5).

## Slices

### Slice 0 — Core prerequisites

**Addresses:** no audit finding directly; lands the accepted core records that Slices 1b, 3, 4, 5
and 6 stand on. **Records:** CORE-NUMERICS-20, CORE-NUMERICS-04, CORE-NUMERICS-17,
MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-02, and the domain-neutral part of MODELS-ANALYTIC-08.
**Verified:** 2026-11-18 against `40c9036fdc` (source, citations, counts). Not verified: the per-product clock reading in 0a step 12, which of the candidate golden, pin and bit-comparison files in 0a, 0b and 0c hold literals, the bare-field and `.r`/`.q` read counts in 0d, and the step numbers this slice quotes from other slices.

Drafted 2026-11-18 with the same scope rule and method as the other slices. Call-site counts are
grep counts. In the `#### 0d` block the "Record remedy" is the Slice 0 work (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08
d5-7); the Heston domain analysis is kept there for Slice 3 #13b and Slice 5, which own it.
`CREDIT-NN` is short for MODELS-AND-VALUATIONS-CREDIT-NN throughout.

**Progress.** Step 0b (all of CORE-NUMERICS-04 in scope) landed on 2026-11-18 on branch
`feat/models-slice-0-quadrature`: 0b-a in `6d39a15acd`, 0b-b and 0b-c in `8bb23585cd`, each
through `mise run slice-gate`. Only 0b-a moved numbers: base-correlation calibration results
changed in the last digits and the UI fixtures were regenerated; no Rust golden changed. Choices
made where the record is silent: evaluation limits are named constants beside each caller
(100,000 for correlated recovery per CREDIT-10 d5, 1,000,000 for the barrier rebate and CDS
option payoff, and limits that cannot bind before the depth limit for tranches, Hull-White and
CMS); the classical weighted sum is kept for ordinary samples, with the overflow-safe form only
above `f64::MAX / 8`; work-limit, geometric and non-finite failures reuse
`SolverConvergenceFailed` with actual counts. Steps 0a, 0c and 0d are not started.

One core test pins the paired callback order and one pins the old failure diagnostics | 1b variance wings; Slice 6 recovery expectation |

The standalone Feller predicate errors on invalid input and stops reporting true through overflow; rough-Heston restoration starts rejecting payloads `HestonParams::new` rejects | 3 (Heston), 5 |

None of 0a, 0b or 0c has a Python, WASM, stub or parity surface, except that 0c-3 makes the Python
`should_pik` raise instead of return, and 0d changes the published `heston_satisfies_feller`.

Corrections this draft makes to the rest of the plan:

- **Heston domain moves to Slice 3.** Admitting `v0 = 0` and `sigma_v = 0` is not in Slice 0.
  Widening `HestonParams::validate` alone is a no-op (Fourier pricing has a second gate) and a
  stopgap (the zero-volatility branches of MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d9-10 and -09 d5 must land with it).
  It belongs in Slice 3 #13b together with those branches and the owner gates of 08 d20.
- **Slice 1b is not blocked by the Heston domain.** MODELS-ANALYTIC-12 d1 gives rough Heston its
  own strictly positive fields.
- **Slice 6 needs 0a for two steps.** Its text cites CREDIT-05 d8 and CREDIT-11 d6 without
  CORE-NUMERICS-20, but CREDIT-11 d6 names CORE-NUMERICS-20 uniform knots and CREDIT-05 d8 links
  the time-grid record for its canonical uniform grid. #7 step 5 and #22 step 5 should build with
  `TimeGrid::uniform` instead of repeating the arithmetic.
- **Re-pin once.** Create the Slice 6 portfolio-loss goldens after 0c-3, so they are pinned once.
- **Records with no owning slice:** MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 and -09 (CIR and Heston QE), the bridge
  and Poisson decisions of CORE-NUMERICS-17 (d7-15), and the replacement for `map_date_to_step`
  in the convertible valuator (CORE-NUMERICS-20 d12).

#### 0b Checked quadrature budgets and measures (CORE-NUMERICS-04)

- **Needed by:**
  - Slice 1b variance wings: MODELS-ANALYTIC-21 d16 ("the accepted scalar adaptive Simpson rule"; an unrepresentable interior split is a computation error) and d17 (separate tolerances, explicit max depth and callback evaluation limit per initial panel; "Apply CORE-NUMERICS-04's checked resource and convergence rules"; exhaustion or estimator failure errors). These reach CORE-NUMERICS-04 d2, d4, d7, d9, d14.
  - Slice 6 recovery expectation (deferred there): CREDIT-10 d5 (checked adaptive Simpson, absolute tolerance `1e-12`, depth 24, at most 100,000 integrand evaluations per expectation; reject invalid estimates and work exhaustion). Reaches CORE-NUMERICS-04 d2, d4, d7.
  - The plan cites no slice that needs the paired rule, Legendre or Hermite directly. They enter through the scope rule (same decisions re-signature them) and through forced consumer reconciliation.

- **State today (verified in source, `finstack-quant/core/src/math/integration.rs`):**
  - d1 families and orders: LANDED. Simpson, adaptive Simpson scalar and pair, Legendre 2/4/8/16 composite, adaptive and grid, Hermite 5/7/10/15/20; unsupported order and bad counts error (`gl_nodes_weights`, `GaussHermiteQuadrature::new`).
  - d2 fallible callbacks and results: NOT LANDED. `simpson_rule` and `gauss_legendre_integrate_composite` take `Fn(f64) -> f64`; `adaptive_simpson` and `gauss_legendre_integrate_adaptive` take `Fn(f64) -> f64 + Copy`; `adaptive_simpson_pair` takes `Fn(f64) -> (f64, f64) + Copy`; `GaussHermiteQuadrature::integrate` returns bare `f64`. `simpson_rule` and the composite Legendre rule can return `Ok(NaN)`; the private `gauss_legendre_integrate` returns `Ok(f64::NAN)` as a sentinel.
  - d3 orientation and zero width: PARTLY. `a == b` returns zero, but only after calling `f(a)`. Adaptive rules run on the reversed interval as given instead of normalizing and negating. Composite Legendre rejects `b <= a` (it goes through `gauss_legendre_grid`).
  - d4 work limits: NOT LANDED. No `max_evals` on any adaptive API; traversal is process-stack recursion to the caller's `max_depth` (a core test passes 200, barrier passes 48); no checked count or integer-to-float conversion.
  - d5 Simpson definitions: LANDED in code (uncorrected two-half value, `/15` proxy, cached endpoint and midpoint samples). The `adaptive_simpson` doc still calls the proxy "Richardson extrapolation".
  - d6 Legendre mapping and estimator: PARTLY. Raw `abs(Q2 - Q1)` and stable `midpoint`/`half_width` landed; child coarse estimates are recomputed, not reused (`recurse` evaluates `i1` for each child again).
  - d7 global estimator budget: PARTLY. Finite nonnegative `tol` and the summed global gate landed. The split is `tol / 2` on both sides, not `left = tol/2, right = tol - left`; totals are plain sums, not checked compensated.
  - d8 pair budgets: NOT LANDED. `adaptive_simpson_pair` takes one `tol` applied to both components.
  - d9 geometry: PARTLY. Midpoint collapse is detected and fails closed, but as a convergence failure rather than a geometric computation error. `gauss_legendre_grid` builds nodes from `(b - a) / panels`.
  - d10 checked accumulation: NOT LANDED outside Hermite. Hermite uses `NeumaierAccumulator` but falls back to a raw sum on accumulator error; Simpson forms `4.0 * f(x)` and `2.0 * f(x)` unscaled.
  - d11 grids: PARTLY (positive-interval, ordered, scaled-once landed; representability, positive-weight and capacity checks not).
  - d12 Hermite measure: LANDED for the measure and raw `get_points`/`get_weights` views; checked scaling and fallible result not.
  - d13 table claims: NOT LANDED. `GaussHermiteQuadrature::new` still documents "Standard option pricing" and "High-precision Heston pricing" by order.
  - d14 truthful diagnostics: NOT LANDED. Failures report configured `max_depth` as `iterations` and the root midpoint as `last_x`; non-finite samples and geometry failures are reported as `SolverConvergenceFailed`.
  - d15 consumer reconciliation: NOT LANDED. Temporary-zero `RefCell` adapters remain in `Copula::try_integrate_fn` (`models/src/correlation/copula/mod.rs`) and in `integrate_interval` and `integrate_normal_pair` (`valuations/credit/src/cds_tranche/pricing/integration.rs`); `StudentTCopula::try_integrate_pair` carries its own `RefCell` error capture around a raw-table loop. The Rust-only surface is LANDED: no Python or WASM binding, stub or facade references quadrature, and `parity_contract.toml` lists `integration` as deliberately unbound.
  - The record's forward-curve Simpson consumer no longer exists: nothing under `finstack-quant/core/src` outside `integration.rs` calls a quadrature function.

- **Record remedy:**
  1. Re-signature every integrator to a fallible callback and result: scalar callbacks `FnMut(f64) -> Result<f64>`, paired `FnMut(f64) -> Result<(f64, f64)>`, no `Copy` bound, borrowed mutable evaluation internally; `GaussHermiteQuadrature::integrate` returns `Result<f64>`. No `try_*` twin and no adapter trait. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d2
  2. Stop at the first callback error and return it with its category unchanged; reject a sampled NaN or infinity instead of returning `Ok(nonfinite)`; delete the `Ok(f64::NAN)` sentinel in the private Legendre panel rule. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d2
  3. Add an explicit positive `max_evals` beside `max_depth` on `adaptive_simpson`, `adaptive_simpson_pair` and `gauss_legendre_integrate_adaptive`. Count actual callback invocations (one paired call counts once). Root is depth 0 and still performs its comparison (5 Simpson calls, `3 * order` Legendre calls). Validate all controls before the first callback; a budget below the root comparison fails; never exceed the budget; no hidden cap or retry. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d4
  4. Replace process-stack recursion with a bounded iterative traversal over an internal stack, left panel before right. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d4, d14
  5. Validate `simpson_rule` `n` (positive, even) and composite `panels` (positive) with checked count and integer-to-float conversion. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d4
  6. Orientation: require finite `a`, `b`; after admitting order, counts, tolerances and work limits, `a == b` returns exact `+0` (or the zero pair) with no callback; for `a > b` run the increasing-bound rule and negate the value, estimator stays nonnegative. Composite Legendre integration accepts reversed and equal bounds under this rule; `gauss_legendre_grid` keeps `a < b`. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d3
  7. Budget split `left = tol / 2`, `right = tol - left`, no epsilon floor; `tol = 0` allowed; sum terminal values and absolute estimates with checked compensated totals; succeed only on a finite value and a finite summed estimate `<=` the original `tol`; depth exhaustion alone is not success; work exhaustion errors with no partial value. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d7
  8. Paired Simpson takes `[tol0, tol1]`, each finite and nonnegative in its own component's units; refine the shared mesh when either component needs it; split, sum and gate each budget independently; on a doubly invalid sample report the first component in tuple order. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d8
  9. Adaptive Legendre reuses each child's known coarse estimate (the parent's half-panel value) instead of evaluating it again, so the callback count under `max_evals` is the final one. Estimator stays `abs(Q2 - Q1)`, value stays `Q2`. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d6
  10. A required midpoint or node that is not finite or collapses onto a panel endpoint is a numerical/geometric failure on a nonzero interval, not zero width and not convergence. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d9
  11. Use the shared Neumaier accumulation for the fixed rules, adaptive value and error totals and each pair component; validate samples, products, partial totals and outputs; scale Simpson weights and the Hermite sum so `4 * f` or the unnormalized sum cannot overflow a representable result; remove Hermite's raw-sum fallback. Done in this step because the same function bodies are rewritten for steps 1-10 and every downstream bit pattern would otherwise move twice. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d10 (adaptive totals are required by d7)
  12. Failure reporting: invalid controls are input/domain errors (`Error::Validation`, as today); an unmet estimator budget is a convergence failure (`InputError::SolverConvergenceFailed`, as today); arithmetic, geometric and work-limit failures are computation errors that name the actual reached depth, actual evaluation count, the limit that stopped the run, and the sampled coordinate or pair component when known. Never report configured `max_depth` or the root midpoint as actual work. Keep scalar and tuple returns; no report type. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d14. Record silent on the variant for the non-convergence computation failures; simplest consistent choice: reuse `SolverConvergenceFailed` with truthful `iterations` (evaluations made), `last_x` (failing coordinate) and a `reason` naming the cause, which already maps to `ErrorKind::Computation` and is how the landed Brent solver reports non-finite objectives and exhausted floats.
  13. Fix the docs on every re-signatured function in the same change (`# Arguments` for `max_evals`, `[tol0, tol1]`, error conditions, doctests); drop "Richardson extrapolation" from `adaptive_simpson`. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d5 (AGENTS.md documentation contract)
  14. Reconcile the copula trait: `Copula::integrate_fn` becomes the one fallible method (fallible callback, `Result<f64>`), `try_integrate_fn` and its `RefCell` temporary-zero adapter are deleted, and callers of `try_integrate_fn` move to `integrate_fn`. Forced by step 1: the Gaussian, RFL and Student-t implementations call `GaussHermiteQuadrature::integrate`, which can no longer produce a bare `f64`. `MultiFactorCopula` and `StudentTCopula::try_integrate_pair` keep their raw-table loops over `get_points`/`get_weights` (and the latter its `RefCell` error capture) for CREDIT-09. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d2, d15
  15. Reconcile the tranche adapters: `integrate_interval` and `integrate_normal_pair` pass their fallible closures straight to `adaptive_simpson` and `adaptive_simpson_pair`, drop the `RefCell` capture, and split their per-segment tolerance and work limit explicitly. The pair call passes the same tolerance for both components (both are portfolio fractions today). CORE-NUMERICS-04 d15, d8
  16. Reconcile the remaining callers mechanically (wrap infallible closures in `Ok`, pass an explicit `max_evals`): `barrier_rebate` discriminant branch, `CorrelatedRecovery::gaussian_expectation` (two calls), `bloomberg_quadrature`, pool `gauss_legendre_integrate_composite`, Hull-White `contractual_swaption`, CMS `replication_pricer`, CMS spread `expected_payoff`. Keep each caller's current tolerance and depth; the barrier branch's `unwrap_or(f64::NAN)` stays for its own record. Callers that integrate several panels own the allocation of an overall work budget. CORE-NUMERICS-04 d15, d4

- **Record remedy:**
  Part A — the type. A and B are one compiling unit: the renames and the builder re-signature break every consumer, so they land in one commit.
  1. One private validator used by every constructor: nonempty, finite, first knot numerically zero and stored as `+0`, strictly increasing; derive every `dt` and the horizon from adjacent knots; delete `MIN_DT`. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d2, d5
  2. `uniform(t_max, num_steps)`: `(0,0) -> [0]`; reject `(0, N>0)`, `(T>0, 0)`, negative and non-finite `T`; knots `t_0 = +0`, `t_N = T`, interior `T * (i as f64 / N as f64)`; pass through the validator, so a collapsed mesh (`uniform(eta, 2)`) is a computation error. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d3, d4, d5
  3. `uniform_with_required_times(t_max, num_steps, required_times)`: validate every event finite and in closed `[0, T]` (reject `nextUp(T)`), normalize zero, validate the base grid first, then union with sort and exact-equality dedup only; no drop, snap, `1e-10` merge or terminal overwrite; zero horizon still validates all events. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d3, d6
  4. Add `get_index(time) -> Result<usize>`: finite in-horizon input, zero normalized, binary search on exact equality; nonmember is a missing-knot input error. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d8
  5. Rename to `get_num_steps`, `get_t_max`, `get_times`, `get_dts` (infallible) and `get_time(i)`, `get_dt(i)` returning `Result<f64>` (nodes `0..=N`, intervals `0..N`); delete the old names. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d9
  6. `is_uniform()` recomputes the d4 profile from stored horizon and count and compares knots exactly; `[0]` is true; no tolerance, no provenance flag. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d10
  7. Admission before allocation: `N <= 2^53 - 1`, checked `N+1` and merged candidate count, byte bounds, fallible reservation; invalid shape is an input error, allocation/resolution failure a computation error. No new public options type. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d11

  Part B — forced consumers (same commit as A).
  8. Update every accessor call site (table below). Record silent on hot loops; simplest consistent choice: read `get_times()` / `get_dts()` once outside the loop and index the slices, and use `get_time` / `get_dt` with `?` only at boundary code. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d9, d14
  9. Move the density-to-count formula `max(round(T * steps_per_year), min_steps)` out of core into `PathDependentPricerConfig::build_time_grid` (its public signature is unchanged), with checked conversion; cliquet and autocallable call `merged_cfg.build_time_grid` instead of passing `steps_per_year` / `min_steps` to core, so the formula has one owner. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d6, d14
  10. Callers that already hold a count pass it directly and stop round-tripping through `count / T`: Bermudan `lsmc_grid` (`base_steps`), Asian `map_fixings_to_steps` (`base_num_steps.max(1)`), both benches. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d6, d14
  11. Replace epsilon knot searches over a `TimeGrid` with `get_index`, keeping one returned index per event: Bermudan `lsmc_grid`, Asian `map_fixings_to_steps`, `Monitoring::time_grid` (relative `1e-12`), `LmmProcess::validate_time_grid` (already exact `binary_search`; use `get_index`). This is behavior-forced, not compiler-forced: once the builder keeps an event one ulp from a base knot, `position(|node| (node - time).abs() < 1e-10)` can return the neighbour. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d7, d8, d14
  12. Out-of-range events now fail the whole build, so each caller must hand over only events on the grid's clock inside `[0, T]`. Read for every production route: autocallable, cliquet, Asian (GBM and Heston), barrier (GBM and Heston), FX barrier and range accrual each compute `T` and every event with the same instrument day count from the same `as_of`, and Bermudan validates `0 < t <= maturity` before the call. An event on the terminal date is therefore bit-identical to `T`, and no production caller depends on today's `1e-10` terminal snap or on the silent drop; only a contractual date after the horizon reaches the new error, and it already fails today at the lookup. No caller clock change is required. CORE-NUMERICS-20 d1, d6, d13

  Not in 0a: d12 helper removal (see Deferred and open question 1). The two helpers are free functions; leaving them compiles and blocks nothing above.

#### 0c PCG and Philox generators with the shared checked Box–Muller transform (CORE-NUMERICS-17, MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-02)

Short forms: CN-17, MC-02. Counts are grep counts over `finstack-quant/`, `finstack-quant-py/src`, `finstack-quant-wasm/src` (`.rs` only, `target/` and `pkg*/` excluded), split prod / test (in-file `#[cfg(test)]`, `tests/`, `benches/`) / doc (`//` lines, doctests).

- **Needed by:**
  - Slice 6 #7 toggle: CREDIT-05 d8 ("one checked CORE-NUMERICS-17 PCG stream per decision", explicit `u64` seed and stream, one common normal per path-step) needs CN-17 d2, d4, d5, d6.
  - Slice 6 #21 portfolio loss: CREDIT-10 d6 cites MC-02 d3 (slot addressing), MC-02 d6 (hash framing) and, through open question 6, MC-02 d4 (reflected Box–Muller). MC-02 d4 delegates the formula to CN-17 d4.
  - No slice cites MC-02 d5 or d2 directly. They enter by the scope rule: CN-17 d4 re-signatures the transform that `PhiloxRng::fill_std_normals` calls and CN-17 d16 names the Philox fill path as a caller to reconcile, so MC-02 d5 is forced; #21 replaces its `PhiloxRng::with_stream` call, so the MC-02 d2 constructor is forced.
  - Slice 4: no citation of MC-02 found in the plan text. #18 step 8 uses an auxiliary Philox stream and #18 step 5 deletes the Sobol `RandomStream` adapter (the plan cites other records there, not read for this part; the deletion is the one MC-02 d8 states).

- **State today (verified in source):**
  - CN-17 d1 (three separate mechanisms, Rust-only): LANDED. No Python or WASM code references `Pcg64Rng`, `RandomNumberGenerator`, `PhiloxRng`, `RandomStream` or the transform.
  - CN-17 d2: NOT LANDED. `Pcg64Rng::new(seed)` goes through `Pcg64::seed_from_u64`; `Pcg64Rng::new_with_stream(seed, stream)` builds state `(stream << 64) | seed` and passes sequence `(stream << 1) | 1`, which pinned `rand_pcg` 0.9.0 shifts again (increment `4*stream + 3`). Docs claim SplitMix64 and non-overlap.
  - CN-17 d3: PARTLY. Behaviour is landed: `uniform` calls `rand` 0.9.5 `StandardUniform`, which is `(word >> 11) * 2^-53` on one `u64` (checked in the pinned source). The stream-independence and "same seed" claims in the module and type docs are not the qualified ones.
  - CN-17 d4: NOT LANDED. `box_muller_transform(u1, u2) -> (f64, f64)` is infallible, clamps `u1` to `[MIN_POSITIVE, 1 - EPSILON]`, uses `ln(u1)` (unreflected) and documents a 37-sigma range.
  - CN-17 d5, d6: NOT LANDED / PARTLY. Trait and PCG `normal(mean, std_dev) -> f64` are infallible, do no parameter check, have no `std_dev == 0` identity and use `mean + std_dev * z`. The cache does hold the standard variate and scales per call.
  - CN-17 d7-d11 bridge: NOT / PARTLY. `BrownianBridge::new(num_steps) -> Self` has no step cap and uses `(left + right) / 2`; `construct_path` validates shape and times but not shock finiteness and evaluates the naive mean and `sqrt(a*b/span)`. Terminal-first depth-first order (d9) is landed.
  - CN-17 d12-d15 Poisson: NOT LANDED. `poisson_inverse_cdf(lambda, u) -> Result<usize>` keeps the `< 30` / 200-count sequential branch and the `statrs` CDF search. The CN-15 log-Gamma tail kernels d13 reuses do not exist (`special_functions.rs` has only `ln_gamma`).
  - CN-17 d16: NOT LANDED for every caller listed under Blast radius.
  - MC-02 d1: LANDED (`PHILOX_M0/M1/W0/W1`, counter and key word layout, ten rounds; `test_philox_matches_random123_reference_vector` pins the all-zero block only).
  - MC-02 d2: NOT LANDED. Two constructors, `PhiloxRng::new(seed)` and `PhiloxRng::with_stream(seed, stream_id)`; the block counter advances with `wrapping_add`, so exhaustion wraps. `substream` and trait `split` already start fresh on the absolute ID.
  - MC-02 d3: LANDED. `fill_u01` returns `(bits + 0.5) * 2^-52` from word pairs `(0,1)`, `(2,3)`; `pub(crate) with_stream_u01_offset` selects block `offset / 2`, pair `offset % 2`, no spare.
  - MC-02 d4: NOT LANDED. Calls the clamped unreflected `box_muller_transform`; `fill_std_normals` takes the spare before testing for an empty buffer, so a zero-length fill discards it.
  - MC-02 d5: NOT LANDED. `RandomStream::{fill_u01, fill_std_normals, next_u01, next_std_normal}` are infallible; no capacity preflight.
  - MC-02 d6: PARTLY. `derive_seed` matches the framing and constants; it takes `(&InstrumentId, &str)` only and `fnv1a_extend` is private.
  - MC-02 d8: NOT LANDED. `impl RandomStream for SobolRng` still exists in `models/src/monte_carlo/rng/sobol.rs`.

- **Record remedy:**
  CN-17 (core PCG and transform)
  1. One constructor `Pcg64Rng::new(seed: u64, stream: u64) -> Self` calling `Pcg64::new(seed as u128, stream as u128)`; delete `new_with_stream`, the `seed_from_u64` route and the state/increment bit mixing; no alias. CN-17 d2
  2. The 5 seed-only production callers pass an explicit stream. Record silent on the value; simplest consistent choice is literal `0`, stated in each caller's docs (mirrors MC-02 d2). Statements `build_rng` keeps its own hashed `(state, increment)` pair as the owner's disclosed stream choice and passes it to `new`. CN-17 d2, d17
  3. Keep `uniform(&mut self) -> f64` infallible and unchanged in value. Rewrite the module, type and constructor docs: period `2^128` per odd increment, no independence or non-overlap claim, replay needs build, dependency and call order, a clone is the same stream. CN-17 d3
  4. Rename `box_muller_transform` to `box_muller(u1, u2) -> Result<(f64, f64)>`: reject non-finite or out-of-`[0,1)` inputs, `r = sqrt(-2*log1p(-u1))`, `theta = 2*pi*u2`, one `sin_cos`, return `(r*cos, r*sin)`, require finite outputs; no clamp, no redraw, no unchecked twin. Remove the 37-sigma text. CN-17 d4
  5. `RandomNumberGenerator::normal(mean, std_dev) -> Result<f64>`: validate finite `mean` and finite `std_dev >= 0` before touching state; `std_dev == 0` returns `mean` with no draw and no cache change; otherwise take one standard variate and return fused `std_dev*z + mean`, erroring on a non-finite result. Parameter errors are input errors, range errors are computation errors. CN-17 d5
  6. Cache rules: no spare consumes exactly two `uniform` calls and caches the second standard variate; a spare consumes none; `uniform` calls leave the spare intact; a failed restoration still consumes its variate and keeps the companion; no rollback or retry. CN-17 d6
  7. Propagate the fallible `normal` through every caller with no unwrap, sentinel or skipped path: `sample_gamma_with_max_attempts` (core), the four statements forecast functions, `mc_breach_probabilities` (covenants, becomes `Result`), Merton `simulate_paths` (two closure sites), bond `merton_mc` `price_terms`, and toggle `nested_equity_mc` -> `optimal_toggle_decision` -> `ToggleExerciseModel::should_pik`, which becomes `Result<bool>` in Rust and raising in Python (WASM already returns `Result`). CN-17 d16
  MC-02 (Philox)
  8. One constructor `PhiloxRng::new(seed: u64, stream_id: u64) -> Self`; delete `with_stream`; seed-only callers pass `0`. Keep `substream`, `split` and `pub(crate) with_stream_u01_offset` (record silent on having both `substream` and `split`; leave both). MC-02 d2, d3
  9. Replace the wrapping counter with a cursor that can represent "no raw slot left" after block `u64::MAX`; a request needing more raw slots fails; no wrap, reseed or substitute. MC-02 d2
  10. `fill_u01`, `fill_std_normals`, `next_u01`, `next_std_normal` return `Result`. Bulk requests preflight with widened counts: `n` normals with `s` cached need `2*ceil(max(n-s,0)/2)` raw uniforms; a capacity failure leaves cursor, spare and output unchanged; an exhausted stream still delivers its spare. MC-02 d5
  11. Normal fill: spare first, then pairs through core `box_muller`, keep the unused second variate; a zero-length fill changes neither cursor nor spare; a failed transform consumes its pair and keeps no companion. MC-02 d4, d5
  12. Reconcile every `RandomStream` implementor and caller (lists below). The 12 helpers that are infallible today gain `Result`; all of their callers already return `Result`. Preserve operation and path context at the caller boundary. MC-02 d5
  13. `impl RandomStream for SobolRng`: adapt it to the new signatures only (two forwarding bodies); Slice 4 #18 step 5 deletes it, and pulling that step forward is the alternative. MC-02 d8

#### 0d Shared `HestonParams` domain (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4-d7, d9-d10, d20-d21; MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d2, d8, d13; MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-09 d1, d9; MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d2; MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d3, d5; MODELS-ANALYTIC-12 d1, d3, d25)

**Scope.** Slice 0 does only MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5-7 (with the shared Feller comparator of MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d2): the bullets from "Record remedy" to "Open questions for the owner" below. The domain change (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4, d9-10, d20; MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d5) is Slice 3 #13b and MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d8, d13 is Slice 5. The three answers, the "State today" consumer table and the closing "Analysis handed to Slice 3 #13b and Slice 5" block are kept for those slices and are not Slice 0 work.

**The three answers first (domain analysis, handed off).**

1. **One type domain, yes.** MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4, MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d2 and MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-09 d1 state the same set: finite `v0 >= 0`, `kappa > 0`, `theta > 0`, `sigma_v >= 0`, `-1 < rho < 1`; Feller violation admitted; `kappa = 0`, `theta = 0` and `|rho| = 1` stay out. That is the domain of the one shared type. The records differ only in what each *consumer* admits on top of it:
   - Rough Heston Fourier is strict: finite positive `v0`, `kappa`, `theta`, `sigma_v`, open `rho`, `H` in (0, 1/2), six flat fields of its own; "the broader classical parameter domain in record 08 does not determine rough Fourier admission" (MODELS-ANALYTIC-12 d1, d3).
   - Rough Monte Carlo "retains its own discretization and parameter domain" (MODELS-ANALYTIC-12 d25; MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d20 keeps it strictly positive). MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-15 was not read in full; cite it before changing that owner.
   - CIR is a different type (`CirParams`) with a wider domain: `theta >= 0`, `sigma >= 0`, `x >= 0` (MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d1). It shares only the Feller comparator, whose standalone domain is `kappa > 0`, `theta >= 0`, `sigma_v >= 0` so that CIR's `theta = 0` cases fit (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d7, MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d2).
   - MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d20 is transitional, not a contradiction: classical MC, PDE and rough keep strictly positive `v0` and `sigma_v` "until those owners independently qualify broader inputs", with an owner admission check "wherever the old constructor supplied those checks". The MC owner (MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-09 d1) and the PDE owner (MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d2) have since selected the wide domain, each conditional on its own exact branch.
2. **Consumers and branches** are in the table under "State today". In short: the Fourier branches (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d9-d10, MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d5) must land with the domain change; the PDE branch (MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d13, plus d8 for `v0 = 0`) belongs to Slice 5; the QE branch (MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-09 d9) has no slice; Slice 1b needs no widening at all.
3. **Widening alone is a stopgap, so the domain change should fold into Slice 3's Heston sub-slice (#13b), not Slice 0.** Changing `HestonParams::validate` by itself does one of two things. With the owner gates d20 requires, every consumer still rejects both zeros (Fourier through its own second gate), so it is a no-op that adds four gates. Without them, zeros reach code no record sanctions: the QE `1e-10` cutoff with trapezoidal variance, an ADI march with zero variance diffusion, and the rough solvers. What can stay in Slice 0 is the domain-neutral part: the immutable type and the Feller diagnostic (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5-d7).

  - Slice 5 #12 step 15: cites MODELS-NUMERICAL-017 d2, d4 and MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4-6 (`HestonPde` built from the checked `HestonPricingParams`, domain `v0 >= 0`, `sigma_v >= 0`). Its Deferred list puts 017 d9-13 out of scope while its open question 2 says d13 must land with the admission: the two lines conflict.
  - Slice 1b #17b step 1: MODELS-ANALYTIC-12 d1, d3. It replaces the embedded `HestonParams` with six strictly checked flat fields, so it is not blocked by this item.
  - Slice 4: cites MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-15 for the rough hybrid scheme only.

- **State today (verified in source):**
  - One type only. `struct HestonParams` exists once, in `finstack-quant/models/src/volatility/heston.rs`; the older "two distinct `HestonParams`" note is stale. `HestonPricingParams`, `RoughHestonFourierParams` and `RoughHestonParams` each embed it as a flattened `pub model`.
  - Domain (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4): NOT LANDED. `HestonParams::validate` rejects `v0 <= 0` and `sigma_v <= 0`; `kappa`, `theta`, `rho` already match.
  - Immutable type (08 d5): PARTLY. Strict restoration is there (`RawHestonParams` with `deny_unknown_fields`, `try_from` through `new`). Still present: five `pub` fields, `impl Default` with economic values, public `validate` for literal-built values, and 26 struct literals that bypass `new`, one of them in production (`From<RawRoughHestonParams>`, which uses serde `from`, not `try_from`).
  - Pricing wrapper (08 d6): PARTLY. `HestonPricingParams` validates finite `r`, `q` and has a strict flat raw type; fields are `pub`, and `new` takes seven scalars instead of rates plus the checked model.
  - Feller diagnostic (08 d7, MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d2): PARTLY. Inclusive, and already shared with `CirParams::satisfies_feller`. Not landed: `heston_satisfies_feller` computes `2.0 * kappa * theta >= sigma_v * sigma_v` (overflows; the record's `kappa = 2^1023` example reports true), is infallible and unvalidated, `HestonParams::new` and `CirProcess::new` log warnings, and `require_feller` survives with test-only callers.
  - Integrated-variance weights (08 d10): NOT LANDED. Public `deterministic_avg_variance` uses the `theta + (v0 - theta)·A` form the record's reference case shows rounding to zero; 5 call sites, all in `closed_form/heston/tests.rs`.

No Heston calibration target exists in `finstack-quant/calibration` (one test string only).
  - Hosts: no Python or WASM class wraps the type. `heston_price` and `price_heston` take `HestonPricingParams` as JSON through serde, so the Rust domain is the host domain. `heston_satisfies_feller` / `hestonSatisfiesFeller` are exported as infallible booleans.

- **Record remedy:**

  No domain change: everything compiles and behaves as today except the Feller predicate. Steps 6-16 are in the hand-off block at the end of 0d.
  1. Make `HestonParams` immutable: private fields, read-only accessors, checked `new` as the only construction, no `Default`, no public `validate`; restoration keeps rejecting missing, duplicate and unknown fields. Move every literal and field mutation through `new`. MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5
  2. `RoughHestonParams` restores through validated construction (`try_from`, not the unvalidated `From` literal). Forced by step 1: this is the one production struct literal. MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5 (d20 states the same for the embedded rough model)
  3. `HestonPricingParams`: private `r`, `q`, `model` with accessors; construct from the two rates and a checked `HestonParams`; flat wire fields unchanged. MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d6
  4. Feller: one range-safe exact comparison of `2·kappa·theta >= sigma_v²` on represented inputs, equality included, no overflow-produced `true`. The standalone Rust and host predicate requires finite `kappa > 0`, `theta >= 0`, `sigma_v >= 0` and returns a checked Boolean (`Result<bool>`); invalid input is a validation error. `HestonParams::satisfies_feller` stays infallible on the already valid type. Delete the constructor warning, `require_feller` and the `CirProcess::new` warning; `CirParams` keeps calling the shared comparator. MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d7, MODELS-MONTE-CARLO-08 d2
  5. Hosts: Python `heston_satisfies_feller` and WASM `hestonSatisfiesFeller` raise on invalid input (`ValueError`: `Validation` maps to it in `finstack-quant-py/src/errors.rs`); stubs and JSDoc follow; the two parity vectors use valid inputs and keep their values. MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d7 (d21 is the general host rule)

All under `docs/decisions/financial/models-analytic/`; IDs confirmed from headers.

Not verified: the reach estimate in the #13b defect line is leading-order algebra, nothing was run; the owner records for the classical MC, PDE and rough-Heston zero-input branches (outside `models-analytic/{08,09}`) were not read; the "about 38 public items" figure is an enumeration of the steps, not a grep.

Scope test used throughout: a decision is in scope if (a) it is the record's answer to the audited defect, or (b) it deletes/renames/re-signatures a function or type the fix edits. A decision is deferred only if nothing built here is deleted or re-signatured when it lands later.

#### #13a COS call pricing and negative-price floor (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source, audit partly wrong):** `finstack-quant/models/src/fourier/cos.rs` does NOT integrate the call directly. `CosPricer::price_strip` already prices the put and recovers the call by parity using `cf(-i).re` with no check on the imaginary part; `cos_finite_price` returns `raw.max(0.0)`; a negative raw put only logs `tracing::warn!`; `truncation_range` errors on a radicand `<= 1e-12` instead of pricing the deterministic case. So the old plan's "put + parity" remedy is roughly the current code. The residue is the floor, the warn-only recovery and the unchecked real part at −i.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Keep `bs_cos_price`, `merton_jump_cos_price`, `vg_cos_price` as the only entry points over one internal typed model path; delete public `CosPricer`, `price_strip`, the strip cache and `&dyn CharacteristicFunction` pricing. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d1
  2. Replace the law layer: one private prepared-law interface per request supplying support facts, c1/c2/c4, centered log moments, real-frequency centered log transforms and a modulus envelope, for the cash put law (s=0, eta=+1) and the share-reflected call law (s=1, eta=−1). Delete the public `CharacteristicFunction` trait, `BlackScholesCf`, `MertonJumpCf`, `VarianceGammaCf` and public `Cumulants`; prepared laws hold no r, q, spot or strike. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-11 d1, d2, d3
  3. Law admission and formulas per model: Black sigma>=0 with exact zero (11 d4, d8); Merton nonnegative sigma/lambda/sigma_jump, compensated cash law, share-measure transform of intensity and jump mean, cumulants, centered log moment, centered log CF, support/atoms (11 d5, d9–d14); VG strict martingale domain D(1)>0 with no tolerance band, selected-coordinate A/B, cumulants, centered log moment, fixed log branch (11 d6, d15–d19); singular laws only from exact inputs (11 d7); envelopes (11 d20); exact origins and explicit computation errors, no NaN/clipping/moment floor (11 d21). — MODELS-ANALYTIC-11 as cited
  4. `CosConfig` becomes validated and immutable with `initial_terms` in [2, 65536], finite positive `initial_l`, finite positive `price_abs_tol`; remove `Default`; remove `CosMarketParams.n_terms`; each scalar takes a required final `&CosConfig`. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d2
  5. Validate the whole request first: finite S>0, K>0, finite T>=0, finite signed r and q, explicit call/put; strict serde with required fields and unknown/duplicate rejection. T=0 is admitted (today it is rejected). — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d3
  6. Normalise to F=S exp((r−q)T), X=log(S_T/F), m=log(K/F), A=S exp(−qT), B=K exp(−rT); use the law's first-moment identity; delete the `cf(-i).re` extraction. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d4, d6
  7. Price the requested side directly: put uses Y=X, kink k=m, scale M=B; call uses Y=−X under the share measure, kink k=−m, scale M=A; both evaluate M·E[(1−exp(Y−k))^+]. No parity in the pricing path. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d5
  8. Analytic branches before any series: T=0 gives spot intrinsic; qualified deterministic law gives discounted forward intrinsic; structural support gives exact zero or discounted intrinsic. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d7, d8
  9. Window, basis and coefficients: h=L·sqrt(c2+sqrt(c4)) as a seed only, no `1e-12` radicand floor; centered coordinates W=2h; clipped payoff integral ell, t; stable V_n with expm1/sinc forms; compensated sum of C_n V_n. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d9–d12
  10. Acceptance: reflected-payoff window bound, two-sided Chernoff probe (64 halvings), window doubling until E_W<=tau/4 (64 candidates), series bound E_N=4W·H(3N/W)/(9(N−1)), term doubling to 65,536 with E_N<=tau/4, conservative enclosures, one final spectrum. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d13–d19
  11. Gate the raw price: R=64·2^-52·mass; require E_W+E_N+R<=tau and <p_hat; raw estimate between normalised discounted intrinsic and one; restore native price with no clipping. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d20
  12. Errors: validation for inputs/controls, computation for transform/coefficient/enclosure failures, the existing convergence category for exhausted search or unresolved raw price; delete `cos_finite_price`'s floor, `numerical_negativity_tolerance` and the warn-only path. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d21
  13. Hosts: same market, model and required config in Rust/Python/WASM. `CosConfig` is not bound in either host today (hosts set only `n_terms` inside the market object), so each of the three host functions gains a required config object argument, carried the way `CosMarketParams` is. Update schema, exports, parity, declarations and notebook calls that set `n_terms`; remove raw-CF schema registrations. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-10 d2, d23, MODELS-ANALYTIC-11 d22
- **Differs from the old plan:** The old plan priced the call as put + parity and added an `Im φ(−i)` check; the record prices the call directly in the share measure (10 d5), retires the −i extraction altogether (10 d4) and keeps parity only as a test identity (10 d22). Removing the floor cannot be done alone: the only non-invented acceptance rule is 10 d20, which needs d13–d19, and the share law needs record 11, so the whole pair is in scope.
- **Files:** Rust `finstack-quant/models/src/fourier/{cos.rs,mod.rs}`, `fourier/characteristic_function/{mod.rs,black_scholes.rs,merton.rs,variance_gamma.rs}`, `models/src/schema.rs`, `models/benches/fourier_pricing.rs` / Python `finstack-quant-py/src/bindings/models/fourier.rs`, `finstack_quant/models/_fourier.pyi` / WASM `finstack-quant-wasm/src/api/models/fourier.rs`, `exports/models.js`, generated `index.d.ts` and `facade-surface.json` / parity `finstack-quant-py/parity_contract.toml` (models comment block naming `CosPricer`/`CosConfig`) / schema `models/schemas/models/1/{cos_config,cos_market_params}.schema.json` rewritten, `{black_scholes_cf,merton_jump_cf,variance_gamma_cf}.schema.json` removed, `schemas/index.json` / callers: none in `finstack-quant/valuations/**`.
- **Tests:** Independent analytic Black, Poisson-mixture and VG references on both sides, exact singular/support cases, reflected call, narrow geometry, tiny prices, work-cap refusal, host/error parity (10 d24, 11 d24). Parity C−P=A−B on independently priced sides (10 d22). Pinned to old behaviour: in-file `cos_strip_matches_singles`, `cos_term_count_must_be_in_range`, `cos_degenerate_cumulants_return_error`, `cos_entry_points_are_bit_stable`; `models/tests/model_audit_regressions.rs`; `finstack-quant-py/tests/{test_models_wasm_parity.py,test_error_kinds.py,models/test_audit_regressions.py}`; `finstack-quant-wasm/tests/wasm_models_closed_form.rs`, `tests/facade/{boundary_input,models_input_boundaries,error_kinds,models_parity,models_audit}.test.mjs`; notebooks `02_pricing/instruments/{fourier_pricing,equity_and_options}.ipynb`; `docs-site/content/learn/2/2.4-*.mdx`.

#### #13b Heston characteristic function false overflow (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source, location differs from audit):** There is no `char_func`/`heston_integrand` and no `closed_form/heston.rs` file. The function is `heston_pj_characteristic_function` in `finstack-quant/models/src/volatility/heston.rs`. With `b_1 = kappa − rho·sigma_v < 0` and phi near 0, `bm + d` tends to 0, so the `HESTON_G_DENOM_EPS = 1e-8` relative guard returns `(0, HestonCfStatus::Overflow)`. `closed_form/heston/strip_pricer.rs` sets `HESTON_STRIP_MAX_CORRUPT_FRACTION = 0.0`, so one such node fails that grid; `heston_call_prices_fourier` in `closed_form/heston/fourier_prices.rs` discards the error, tries up to six wider/finer grids and then reports non-convergence. Reach is narrow: to leading order the guarded quantity is `phi·sigma_v²/(2|b_1|)`, so the guard trips only for phi below about `2e-8·|b_1|(1+2|b_1|)/sigma_v²` (about 1.25e-8 for the record's reference case kappa=0.125, sigma_v=0.5, rho=0.5, which the record trips at u=1e-12). The default grid (one frequency unit per 16-point panel) has its first node near 5e-3, and the sixth grid near 1.7e-4. With `settings = None`, which is all the Python and WASM `heston_price` wrappers and `valuations/equity/src/equity_option/pricing/mod.rs` pass, a trip therefore needs sigma_v below roughly 1e-4 together with kappa < rho·sigma_v; otherwise it needs a dense caller-supplied grid through the public Rust `HestonFourierSettings`, or a direct call to the public function. The audit's fix (re-orient `Re(d)`) contradicts the record, which keeps the positive-real-part root.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Replace the function with a crate-private typed `heston_log_cf` returning `Result<Complex64>`: the affine logarithm L, finite, with nonpositive real part. Delete `HestonCfStatus`, the zero sentinels and `HESTON_EXPONENT_REAL_LIMIT`. Invalid inputs are validation errors; unresolved range/phase/bound are computation errors. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d17
  2. Take a crate-private cash/share contour selector and a finite real u of either sign; remove the `j: u8` selector and its implicit P2 default. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d3
  3. Evaluate in the carry-normalised coordinate X_T=log(S_T/F); no spot, strike, carry or discount enters the evaluator. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d2
  4. u=0 or T=0 returns L=0 on either contour after admission. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d8
  5. Riccati constants b_1=kappa−rho·sigma_v, b_2=kappa, a_1=+1/2, a_2=−1/2, Q=−u²+2i·a_j·u, B=b_j−i·rho·sigma_v·u; the same kappa·theta on both contours for b_1<=0. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d11
  6. d=sqrt(B²−sigma_v²·Q) with positive real part, computed without cancelling the positive part. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d12
  7. Decaying form without g: J=−expm1(−dT)/d, Z=1+(B−d)J/2 (or exp(−dT)+(B+d)J/2), D(T)=QJ/(2Z), A_c(T)=kappa·theta[(B−d)T−2·Log(Z)]/sigma_v²; rationalise a cancelling B∓d through their product sigma_v²·Q; delete `HESTON_G_DENOM_EPS`. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d13
  8. Principal Log of the full ratio Z; unresolved branch geometry is a computation error. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d14
  9. Complex expm1 and log1p remainders for short time and small sigma_v; A_c numerator as (B−d)(T−J)+2[W−Log(1+W)] with W=(B−d)J/2; no substitution of the deterministic formula. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d15
  10. Callers: `strip_pricer.rs` (production strip and the test-only `heston_pj_with_diagnostics`/`heston_pj_on_grid`) use the checked log, restore the phase with log(F/K) at the established sign, and propagate errors instead of counting corrupted nodes; `HestonStripPricer::new` stops swallowing failures into `None`, and the six-grid loop in `heston_call_prices_fourier` returns a hard log-CF error immediately instead of treating it as an unresolved grid (its retry on finite unresolved tail/mesh diagnostics stays). — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d18, d19; MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d21

  *Parameter-domain change (steps 11–18 land together, after Slice 0 step 0d). Admitting the two zeros without their branches would either be a no-op behind the owner gates or let zeros into code no record sanctions; and the form installed by step 7 is 0/0 at sigma_v = 0, so the exact branch is structurally required once the domain widens.*
  11. Widen `HestonParams::new` to finite v0 >= 0 and sigma_v >= 0; kappa > 0, theta > 0 and −1 < rho < 1 are unchanged, and kappa = 0, theta = 0 and |rho| = 1 stay out. No perturbation or floor moves a boundary value into the domain. The evaluator requires finite T >= 0. Depends on Slice 0 step 0d: once the fields are private, `new` is the only construction path. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4
  12. Remove the second gate. `validate_heston_inputs` in `closed_form/heston/fourier_prices.rs` (called from `heston_call_prices_fourier` and from `HestonStripPricer::new`) re-checks v0, kappa, theta and sigma_v as finite and positive and rho as open, independently of `HestonParams`; left alone it would reject both zeros after step 11. Delete its model-field checks and keep the request checks: finite positive spot and strike, finite T >= 0. Negative time becomes a validation error (today `time <= 0` returns intrinsic). The whole request is validated before the T = 0 and sigma_v = 0 branches. Safe only after Slice 0 step 0d: today the `pub` fields let a literal bypass `new`, and this gate is the only check such a value meets. The discount-range check in the same function stays until 09 d6 lands. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d3
  13. Private integrated variance I = T[v0·A(x) + theta·B(x)], x = kappa·T, A(x) = −expm1(−x)/x, B(x) = (x + expm1(−x))/x, A(0) = 1, B(0) = 0, with the two nonnegative weights evaluated directly. Delete the public `HestonParams::deterministic_avg_variance`, whose `theta + (v0 − theta)·A` form rounds to zero in the record's reference case; its five call sites are all in `closed_form/heston/tests.rs`. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d10
  14. `heston_log_cf` at literal sigma_v = 0 returns (−u²/2 + i·a_j·u)·I, taken before the root of step 6. Every positive sigma_v uses the stochastic law, with no substitution of this branch for small values. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d9, d12, d15
  15. Price at sigma_v = 0 and T > 0: the checked discounted Black price with total standard deviation sqrt(I), returned before any grid is resolved (`resolve_heston_settings` divides by sigma_v). Use the checked Black kernel, not `bs_price_unchecked`. No Gaussian branch for small positive sigma_v, Feller status or a failed integration. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d5
  16. v0 = 0 with sigma_v > 0 at T > 0 uses the stochastic transform; the zero start supplies no shortcut. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d8. Record silent on the current cutoff seed, which 09 d15 retires: `HestonFourierSettings::for_maturity` returns a NaN `u_max` for v0 = 0, and `resolve_heston_settings` ends with a finite cutoff only because `f64::max` drops the NaN in favour of the large-frequency term `sqrt(1−rho²)(v0 + kappa·theta·T)/sigma_v`, which is itself finite and positive at v0 = 0. Simplest consistent choice if record 09 stays out: seed from that term alone when v0 = 0, with the strip's existing tail gates deciding acceptance. No Fourier gate on v0 > 0: 08 d20 does not list the Fourier path among the owners that may stay strict. See open question 3.
  17. The characteristic function itself is not bound in either host and has no valuations caller.

#### #14 Forward and spot Greek conventions, boundaries and discounting (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `closed_form/forward.rs`: `black76_price` takes and applies `df`, while `black76_greeks`, `bachelier_price`, `black_shifted_price` are unit-scale; `black_shifted_vega` is vega-only; the Greek functions return `Result<ForwardGreeks>` at T=0/vol=0 with a fabricated delta of 1 or 0 and zero gamma/vega (the `None` branches of the leaves in `black.rs`/`bachelier.rs`; the forward leaves have no half-delta). `closed_form/vanilla.rs`: `bs_greeks` divides vega/rhos by 100, takes `theta_days_per_year`, and rejects T=0 or vol=0 (while `bs_greeks_unchecked` returns a strict exercise-indicator delta and zeros at expiry), so `option_calculation` (`calculation.rs`) errors for Black–Scholes at the boundary but not for the forward models. `validate_black_shifted` already accepts a signed shift.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. One unit scale: remove `df` from `black76_price`; delete `bachelier_price_with_annuity`; owners apply discount or annuity and notional once. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d2
  2. Validate before any shortcut: all finite; Black F,K>0; normal any sign; T>=0, vol>=0; shifted Black takes a finite SIGNED s with F+s, K+s>0. Do not add a `shift >= 0` rule. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d3
  3. T=0 or vol=0 price is max(F−K,0)/max(K−F,0) from the original coordinates (shift cancels). — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d5
  4. Native partials on the unit premium: delta Φ(d1)/−Φ(−d1) (normal Φ(d)/−Φ(−d)), gamma φ(d1)/(G·w) (normal φ(d)/u), vega G·sqrt(T)·φ(d1) (normal sqrt(T)·φ(d)); vega per 1.0 of the model vol. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d8
  5. `black76_greeks`, `bachelier_greeks` and the new shifted bundle return `Result<Option<ForwardGreeks>>`; `None` only for valid T=0 or vol=0; regular requests return the whole bundle or a computation error; no fabricated boundary delta or zero gamma/vega. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d9
  6. Replace `black_shifted_vega` with `black_shifted_greeks(forward, strike, vol, expiry, shift, option_type)` returning the same three-field bundle. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-02 d16

Native spot units: remove `/100` from vega and both rhos and remove `theta_days_per_year` from the kernel, request and hosts (delete `DEFAULT_THETA_DAYS_PER_YEAR` and `ONE_PERCENT` from `closed_form`); theta is −∂V/∂T per model year; formulas per the d10 table. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-01 d10, d11, d12

#### #15 Seasoned lookback extremum clamp and inverted stub docstring (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** The four leaves in `closed_form/lookback.rs` clamp silently (`spot_max.max(spot)`, `spot_min.min(spot)`) and return `NaN` on invalid inputs; `lookback_option_price` in `closed_form/dispatch.rs` only requires a positive finite extremum. `finstack_quant/models/_closed_form.pyi` documents `extremum` as "min for call, max for put", which is right for floating strike only and wrong for the default `strike_type="fixed"`. The WASM JSDoc already states the four-way map correctly.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. One checked request: `lookback_option_price(params: &LookbackPriceParams) -> Result<f64>` with required spot, time, carry, discount, volatility, extremum, option_type and strike; spot and extremum finite positive; time and volatility finite nonnegative; validate everything before any shortcut. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d2
  2. `strike` is required and nullable: `Some(K)` with finite K>0 is fixed strike, explicit `None`/null is floating, missing is invalid. Delete the `strike_type` string, `DEFAULT_LOOKBACK_STRIKE_TYPE`, the ignored floating strike and the four public leaves (`fixed_strike_lookback_call/put`, `floating_strike_lookback_call/put` become private kernels). — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d3
  3. Fixed call and floating put require maximum M>=S; fixed put and floating call require minimum 0<m<=S; equality is valid; an inconsistent extremum is a validation error. Delete the clamps. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d4
  4. Take finite signed carry b and an independent finite discount D>0 (D>1 allowed) in place of rate and dividend yield; the kernels use b and D as supplied. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d5
  5. Payoffs: fixed call (M_T−K)^+, fixed put (K−m_T)^+, floating call S_T−m_T, floating put M_T−S_T. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d6
  6. T=0 evaluates the payoff with S_T=S and the supplied extremum, times D; sigma=0 uses S_T=S·exp(bT) in the applicable extremum, times D once. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d7, d8
  7. Regular law: reflection premium R_d(H) with a=d·log(H/S), B=bT, w=sigma·sqrt(T); assemble fixed call D(M−K)^+ + C(H) + R_+(H) with H=max(K,M), fixed put D(K−m)^+ + P(H) + R_−(H) with H=min(K,m), floating call C(m)+R_−(m), floating put P(M)+R_+(M); exact nonzero-carry divided difference for b≠0; analytic limit D·S·w[φ(x)+xΦ(x)] only for b=0; delete `RATE_EQ_DIV_TOL = 1e-7`; stable evaluation through log-CDF/expm1 geometry. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d9–d13
  8. Return a finite nonnegative price or a computation error; delete the `NaN` returns and the final `.clamp(0.0, f64::INFINITY)`. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d14
  9. Hosts: the same required request object in Python and WASM, explicit null for floating, unknown/missing/duplicate fields rejected; rewrite the stub with the correct four-way extremum map; reconcile exports, declarations, schema, parity and the notebook. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d20
  10. `valuations/exotics/src/exotics/lookback_option/pricer.rs` (and the `mod.rs` doc links) move from the four leaves to the request, building b and D from its own r, q and expiry and passing its current-observation-advanced extremum, which 06 d4 permits the dated owner to do. — MODELS-ANALYTIC-06 d3, d4
- **Differs from the old plan:** The validation rule is the same (06 d4). The old plan kept `(market, extremum, strike_type)` and patched the docstring; the record replaces the request (06 d2, d3, d5), so the entry point, both hosts and the valuations caller are re-signatured. The kernels today are written in (r, q) with `df = exp(−rT)`; moving them to (b, D) and then replacing them under d9–d13 would build the same code twice, so d9–d13 are in scope.
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/closed_form/{lookback.rs,dispatch.rs,mod.rs,README.md}`, `models/src/schema.rs` / Python `finstack-quant-py/src/bindings/models/{closed_form.rs,mod.rs}`, `finstack_quant/models/_closed_form.pyi` / WASM `finstack-quant-wasm/src/api/models/closed_form.rs`, `src/api/README.md`, `exports/models.js`, generated `index.d.ts`, `facade-surface.json` / parity `parity_contract.toml` (comment block on `lookback_option_price`) / schema: new `lookback_price_params` schema and index entry / callers `valuations/exotics/src/exotics/lookback_option/{pricer.rs,mod.rs}`.
- **Tests:** Rejection of M<S and m>S on all four payoffs, acceptance at equality, T=0 and sigma=0 singletons with D≠exp(−rT), missing versus null strike in both hosts, error-kind parity. Pinned: in-file `lookback.rs` tests that call the leaves; `models/tests/lookback_commodity_regressions.rs`; the four `analytical_pricer_matches_*_benchmark` tests and `lookback_mc_includes_terminal_step_in_extremum` in the valuations pricer; `finstack-quant-py/tests/models/test_analytical_bindings.py`; `finstack-quant-wasm/tests/facade/models_audit.test.mjs`; notebook `02_pricing/instruments/equity_and_options.ipynb`. Regular-domain prices with b=r−q and D=exp(−rT) should agree with current values to formula-level tolerance except inside the old |b|<1e-7 band; in-file tests that pin the band edge (`RATE_EQ_DIV_TOL`) are re-derived. Bounds of 06 d15 are test identities.

#### Open questions for the owner

1. Scope: read literally, this slice is three package rewrites (COS + laws, Heston transform and parameter domain, forward/spot Greek contract) plus the lookback request, about 38 public Rust items (11 COS and laws, 5 Heston, 15 forward/spot, 7 lookback) and 16 valuations files (10 for #14, 2 for #15, 4 for #13b). Split into 3a (#14 + #15), 3b (#13a, records 10 + 11) and 3c (#13b, record 08 with 09 d3, d5, d21; after Slice 0 step 0d)?
2. #14 leaves: deferring 02 d16/d20 (34 files) and 01 d15 price half (17 files, 11 outside `closed_form`) leaves public leaves that still return a fabricated boundary delta and `NaN`. Accept that until a later slice, or pull them in?
3. Record 09 in #13b. What the records settle: 09 d3 and d5 must come with the domain change and are in (steps 12 and 15); sigma_v = 0 never reaches a grid, so it needs nothing else from 09. Taking only 09 d13–d15 is not a coherent cut: d15 fixes the cutoff U during mesh refinement and removes the sampled tail mass, sampled CF maximum, Gaussian seed and large-frequency slope as cutoff determinants, while the current loop doubles `u_max` on every retry and accepts on exactly those sampled-tail gates; adopting d13–d15 therefore pulls in the mesh and acceptance rules d16–d20, which need the time-value assembly d6–d11, and with them d2 and d22. So the choice is between two things only. (a) Take record 09 d2–d22 and d24 here: `heston_price` loses its `settings` argument; `HestonFourierSettings`, `HestonStripPricer`, the six-grid loop and the public call-only alias `heston_call_price_fourier` go (the alias has callers in three benches, `pde/bridge2d.rs`, `volatility/rough_heston.rs`, `models/tests/vol_models_quantlib_tests.rs`, `models/tests/performance/heston_probabilities.rs` and the test module of `pde2d_pricer.rs`); steps 10 and 16 shrink to the scalar driver, and v0 = 0 is covered by the d13 envelope (it carries the kappa·theta·T²/2 term). Hosts change too: both wrappers already pass no settings, but Python `is_call` and WASM `isCall` default to a call, and 09 d2 and d24 require the choice in every language. d23 (dated owner) and d25 stay separable. (b) Keep 09 out and accept, against the scope test, that the strip edits of step 10 and the v0 = 0 seed of step 16 are built now and deleted when 09 lands. The records do not choose between (a) and (b). Which?
4. #13b severity. The false overflow is real in the public function but, by the estimate in the defect line, the default grids never sample close enough to the origin to trip it for ordinary parameters. Is #13b still wanted at Major priority as a record-08 reconciliation, or re-ranked? The estimate has not been run.
5. `OptionSolve`'s wire tag `implied_vol` (the solve kind, not the result field): record silent; kept.
6. Valuations report units: 01 d11/d20 are read as "equity and FX product vega/rho stay per 1% and theta per day via explicit owner conversion". Confirm.
7. #15: 06 d9–d13 (kernel re-derivation) are placed in scope because the (b, D) request would otherwise force a throwaway re-parameterisation of the current kernels. If you prefer the smaller cut, the alternative is that mechanical re-parameterisation now and d9–d13 later.

### Slice 1a — Surface arbitrage diagnostics and Dupire local volatility

**Addresses:** #1, #2 (Blockers), #9 (Major)  **Records:** MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 (`models-analytic/surface-arbitrage-diagnostics.md`), MODELS-ANALYTIC-19 (`models-analytic/dupire-local-volatility.md`), MODELS-ANALYTIC-16 (`models-analytic/volatility-source-interpolation.md`, d6-7 only, by delegation), CORE-NUMERICS-25 (`core-numerics/contracts-and-diagnostics.md`, d5 only)

**Verified:** 2026-11-18 against `40c9036fdc` (source, citations, counts). Not verified: the audit's own wording quoted in the #1 and #9 defect lines (the audit text is not among the tracked files).

#### #1 `min_severity` filter flips `passed` to true on arbitraged surfaces (Blocker)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `check_surface` in `finstack-quant/models/src/volatility/arbitrage/mod.rs` runs `all_violations.retain(|v| v.severity >= config.min_severity)` before computing `passed`, and `passed` also ignores `Negligible` breaches that already exceeded `config.tolerance`. Counts are built from the filtered vector. The audit names the function `check_surface_arbitrage` and lists `CallMonotonicityCheck` and `PutCallBoundsCheck`; the real entry point is `check_surface` and only `ButterflyCheck`, `CalendarSpreadCheck` and `LocalVolDensityCheck` exist.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Delete `ArbitrageSeverity`, `classify_severity`, `ArbitrageCheckConfig.min_severity`, `ArbitrageReport.passed` and `counts_by_severity`. No severity bands survive, including for display. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d23
  2. Delete `ArbitrageViolation.suggested_fix` and the unused `ArbitrageType::{SviMomentBound, SviButterflyCondition, SviCalendarSpread}` variants. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d23
  3. Add one status enum `Failed | Incomplete | Passed`, derived from the complete report: `Failed` if any selected criterion breached (even with incomplete coverage); `Incomplete` if no breach and any selected check lacked complete coverage of its declared scope; `Passed` only with complete coverage and zero breaches. Apply the same rule per selected family and overall. An operation error is not a status. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d22
  4. Replace `ArbitrageCheckConfig` with a request that selects checks explicitly (at least one required; the whole request is validated before any probe), carries per-comparison thresholds with no defaults (`total_variance_abs_tol`, finite and nonnegative, for the calendar family; the Dupire family takes no tolerance but an explicit nonempty rectangular range of expiry and strike indices, which may be the whole grid), and removes `tolerance`, `DEFAULT_ARBITRAGE_TOLERANCE`, the `Default` impl that enables all checks and the `#[serde(default)]` that fills omitted fields. The request keeps rejecting unknown fields. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d3, d4, d15
  5. Replace `forward_prices: Option<Vec<f64>>` with one finite positive forward bound to each exact surface expiry; delete `expand_forward_prices` (scalar broadcast). Keep the numeric source clock optional. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d2
  6. Keep `check_surface` as the single public entry point on a validated unshifted Black strike surface with positive expiries and strikes (zero sigma is valid). Delete `ButterflyCheck`, `CalendarSpreadCheck`, `LocalVolDensityCheck`, `check_butterfly_grid`, `check_calendar_spread_grid`, `check_local_vol_density_grid` and `check_surface_grid`. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d1
  7. Replace `ArbitrageViolation` with typed findings in native units: the tested inequality, the source expiry and strike (or the paired calendar coordinates), the native signed metric, its threshold and units. Density factor g is dimensionless; total variance and its time derivative are distinct units. Drop the free-text `description` as the carrier of the numbers. The report keeps its selected scope and its source, forward and clock association. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d20
  8. Add per-family coverage to the report: sampled domain, checked counts, unavailable probes with a typed reason and location, density and time-slope probes counted separately, and unselected families distinguished from selected-but-incomplete ones. Findings on partially covered scopes are retained. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d21
  9. Thin grid, zero centre variance or an unsupported matched point is a coverage limitation with a typed reason. Invalid source or request, failed arithmetic or unrepresentable output is a typed error for the whole operation; never an empty findings list. This removes the silent `return violations` early exits and the `continue` on `fwd <= 0.0`. MODELS-ANALYTIC-20 d19
  10. Order findings by fixed check kind, then source order, then probe coordinate (replaces the sort by descending severity). Check work, counts and output sizes before publishing. Restore strictly: `ArbitrageReport` and its finding and coverage types reject unknown fields and deserialize only through a checked raw form (`try_from`, as `LocalVolSurface` already does). Today `ArbitrageReport`, `ArbitrageViolation` and `ViolationLocation` derive raw `Deserialize` and accept unknown fields. The record allows status and counts to be either derived on restore or serialized and verified.
