#### #20 `EndogenousHazardSpec` leverage map and post-PIK lookup (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `finstack-quant/models/src/credit/endogenous_hazard.rs`: `hazard_after_pik_accrual` divides `accreted_notional / asset_value` unchecked. Negative assets give negative leverage, which the power map floors to zero hazard, the exponential map sends toward zero and the table flat-extrapolates to its first knot. Zero assets under positive debt give `+inf` leverage, which a parametric map with positive `h0` and a positive coefficient turns into the `1e6` cap and the table into its last knot. `0/0` gives NaN, which `hazard_at_leverage` returns as NaN. `hazard_at_leverage` clamps every other result to `[0, MAX_HAZARD_RATE = 1.0e6]`. Tabular state stores and round-trips its first knot as `base_leverage` / `base_hazard_rate`.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. `hazard_at_leverage` becomes fallible and requires finite `L >= 0`; delete the negative-leverage floor, the NaN pass-through and `MAX_HAZARD_RATE`. CREDIT-05 d1.
  2. Maps: `h0*(L/L0)^a` and `h0*exp(s*(L − L0))` computed from stable log-ratios as one scaled product. Exact cases after validation: `h0 = 0` gives zero; `L = L0` gives exactly `h0`; a zero coefficient gives the constant `h0` including at `L = 0`; power law at `L = 0` with positive exponent gives zero. An unrepresentable result, or a required positive intensity lost to zero, is an error. CREDIT-05 d1.
  3. Table: equal nonempty axes, finite nonnegative values, strictly increasing leverage in supplied order, single point constant, checked linear interpolation with exact knot values and flat endpoints, nonmonotone hazard axis preserved. CREDIT-05 d2.
  4. A table has no reference pair: drop the derived first-knot `base_*` fields from tabular state and wire, and delete the `to_bits` equality check in `TryFrom<RawEndogenousHazardSpec>`. Record silent on the Rust shape; simplest consistent form is an enum whose parametric variants carry `h0`, `L0` and the coefficient and whose tabular variant carries only the two axes, with `base_hazard_rate()` / `base_leverage()` removed or made parametric-only. CREDIT-05 d2, d10.
  5. `hazard_after_pik_accrual` requires a finite nonnegative already-accreted balance and finite positive same-basis assets, checks the quotient, then evaluates the map; non-positive or non-finite assets are an error, not a hazard value. CREDIT-05 d2.
  6. Propagate the `Result` through the bond toggle feature (`eh.hazard_at_leverage(leverage)` in `merton_mc/engine.rs`) and both host bindings. CREDIT-05 d10.
- **Differs from the old plan:** old plan returned `MAX_HAZARD_RATE` for insolvent firms and NaN for NaN inputs. Record removes the cap entirely and makes both conditions errors.
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/credit/endogenous_hazard.rs`, `credit/README.md`, `models/schemas/models/1/endogenous_hazard_spec.schema.json`. Caller `valuations/fixed-income/src/bond/pricing/merton_mc/{engine.rs,tests.rs}`, `valuations/fixed-income/tests/bond/merton_mc_convergence.rs`. Python `src/bindings/models/credit/mod.rs` (`hazard_at_leverage`, `hazard_after_pik_accrual`, the `base_hazard_rate` / `base_leverage` getters), `finstack_quant/models/credit/__init__.pyi`. WASM `src/api/models/credit/mod.rs` (`hazardAtLeverage`, `hazardAfterPikAccrual`, the same two getters). Parity: `parity_contract.toml` credit comment block. Schema: `instrument_pricing_overrides.schema.json` and embedding schemas via `gen-write`.
- **Tests:** add anchor exactness, zero-coefficient constant at `L = 0`, power law zero at `L = 0`, large-but-representable hazard above `1e6` returned unclipped, overflow is an error, negative/NaN leverage is an error, `asset_value <= 0` is an error, tabular wire without reference fields round-trips and one carrying them is rejected. Existing tests asserting the `1e6` cap, the NaN pass-through or the negative-leverage floor must be re-derived: in `endogenous_hazard.rs`, `zero_hazard_and_zero_sensitivity_do_not_create_nan_on_overflow` (evaluates at `f64::MAX`, `+inf` and NaN leverage) and `exponential_hazard_does_not_overflow_to_infinity` (expects a capped finite value where the record wants an error), plus the deserialization tests that send `base_hazard_rate` / `base_leverage` with a table. Hosts: `finstack-quant-py/tests/models/test_model_contract_regressions.py`, `finstack-quant-py/tests/test_credit_validation.py`, `tests/test_models_credit_rust_owned.py`, `tests/test_models_wasm_parity.py`, `finstack-quant-wasm/tests/wasm_models_credit.rs`, `tests/facade/{models_input_boundaries,models_credit_merton,models_parity}.test.mjs`.
- **Deferred:** CREDIT-05 d3-4 as under #7.

#### #19 CreditGrades PD and Merton zero-coupon spreads (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `finstack-quant/models/src/credit/merton/default_probability.rs`: `credit_grades_default_probability` returns `0.0` for `horizon <= 0`, forms `d = (V/B)*exp(λ²)` (overflows for large `λ`, `inf * 0 = NaN` survives `.clamp(0.0, 1.0)`), and builds `a_t` through `λ²/horizon`. `finstack-quant/models/src/credit/merton/spreads.rs`: `debt_spread` computes `V*exp(−qT) − equity` and takes the log of its ratio to `B*exp(−rT)`; `implied_spread` computes `−ln(1 − pd*lgd)/T`. Upper bound on `barrier_uncertainty` is absent as the audit says; the record does not ask for one. `finstack-quant/models/src/credit/merton/calibration.rs`: `MertonModel::implied_equity` rejects only `JumpDiffusion`, so first-passage and CreditGrades models return an implied equity pair. Hosts disagree on `MertonReport.implied_equity`: Rust and the WASM wire object carry `MertonImpliedEquity { value, vol }` or null, while the Python getter flattens it to a `(value, vol)` tuple and Python has no `MertonImpliedEquity` type. Both host bindings are in `credit/mod.rs` (`finstack-quant-py/src/bindings/models/credit/mod.rs`, `finstack-quant-wasm/src/api/models/credit/mod.rs`).
- **Record remedy:**
  1. CreditGrades survival for finite `ℓ > 0`: `xC = log(V/B) + ℓ²` with `xC >= 0` required, `A = hypot(σ sqrt(T), ℓ)`, `S(T) = Φ(−A/2 + xC/A) − exp(xC) Φ(−A/2 − xC/A)` evaluated as a checked log difference (scaled normal tail for the second term), PD as the complementary positive sum; `xC = 0` gives zero survival. CREDIT-04 d5, d3.
  2. Evaluate the same formula at `T = 0` and keep its origin default mass; delete the `horizon <= 0 → 0.0` branch for CreditGrades. CREDIT-04 d5.
  3. `ℓ = 0` uses the flat first-passage law under zero arithmetic asset drift, including immediate default at `V <= B`; delete the `a_t <= 0` indicator branch. CREDIT-04 d5.
  4. Compute log moneyness without an overflowing ratio (near-equality `log1p` form), scale `σ sqrt(T)` and standardized arguments before squaring or dividing, delete the blanket `.clamp(0.0, 1.0)` on this path; a legitimately rounded endpoint is allowed, an unresolved result is an error. This makes the CreditGrades PD, and therefore `MertonModel::default_probability` / `default_probabilities`, fallible. CREDIT-04 d3.
  5. Separate recoveries: barrier mean recovery `Lbar` in `(0,1]` stays only in the spot-equity helper (`B = D*Lbar`, `V = E + B`, `σ = σE*(E/V)`); direct construction takes the barrier itself; obligation recovery `R` in `[0,1]` enters spreads independently. Remove `mean_recovery` from `AssetDynamics::CreditGrades` state and delete the forced-equality check in `merton/hazard_curve.rs` (`to_hazard_curve`, reached by `cds_par_spread`). CREDIT-04 d5.
  6. `implied_spread`: `−log(1 − PD(T)(1 − R))/T` through `log1p` or the complementary `R + (1 − R) S(T)` form; positive maturity, valid `R`, positive price factor, finite result; zero loss gives zero spread. CREDIT-04 d9.
  7. `debt_spread`: `−log(E_Q[min(V_T/B, 1)])/T` for terminal GBM and terminal jump diffusion, with the normalized factor `Φ(m/s) + exp(m + s²/2) Φ(−(m + s²)/s)` per normal component of log moneyness (mean `m`, deviation `s`), computed in log/scale with the complementary normalized put loss near par. Delete the discounted-assets-minus-equity subtraction and `terminal_equity_value`'s use here. CREDIT-04 d9.
  8. Jump case in step 7 uses the existing component enumeration in this slice (see Deferred and Open questions). CREDIT-04 d9; record's bounded-mixture diagnostic is d4.
  9. Propagate the fallible PD through its callers: `merton/{calibration,hazard_curve,report,spreads,model,mod}.rs`, the bond engine (which stops calling it for the toggle feature under #7 step 10), and both host bindings in `credit/mod.rs`. CREDIT-04 d12 (Rust owns errors; propagate checked PD to the bond PIK trigger).
  10. `MertonReport.implied_equity` (handed over from Slice 7). (a) `MertonModel::implied_equity` admits terminal GBM only: reject a first-passage barrier and CreditGrades as it already rejects jumps, so the report field is unavailable for every other law. CREDIT-04 d6 ("Implied equity value and volatility are jointly available only for terminal GBM"). (b) One host shape for the jointly available pair: Python `MertonReport.implied_equity` returns the Rust-named `MertonImpliedEquity` with `value` and `vol` (new class, stub, `__all__`, parity `types` entry) instead of a tuple, matching the Rust field and the WASM generated type. Record silent on the host shape; the Rust struct is the simplest consistent choice. CREDIT-04 d6, d12 (reconcile Python, WASM, declarations, parity and the UI report). The model method `implied_equity(horizon)` already agrees across hosts (Rust tuple, Python tuple, WASM two-element array) and keeps its shape.
- **Differs from the old plan:** old plan clamped the CreditGrades result into `[0,1]` and used a Black–Scholes put over `B*exp(−rT)` with `ln_1p`. Record removes the clamp, keeps the `T = 0` origin mass, splits `ℓ = 0` to its own law, drops the forced recovery equality, and defines the debt spread by the normalized `min(V_T/B, 1)` factor. The audit's suggested `λ` cap has no basis in the record and is dropped.
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/credit/merton/{default_probability,spreads,dynamics,calibration,hazard_curve,report,model,mod}.rs`. Caller `valuations/fixed-income/src/bond/pricing/merton_mc/engine.rs`. Python `src/bindings/models/credit/mod.rs`, `finstack_quant/models/credit/__init__.{py,pyi}`. WASM `src/api/models/credit/mod.rs` (`creditGrades`, `impliedSpread`, `debtSpread`, `impliedEquity`, PD methods), `exports/models/credit.js`, `tests/wasm_models_credit.rs`, `tests/facade/{models_credit_merton,models_parity}.test.mjs`. Parity: `parity_contract.toml` `[crates.models.credit]` `public` and the `[parity.modules."finstack_quant.models.credit"]` `types` map gain `MertonImpliedEquity` (step 10). Schema (via `gen-write`): `models/schemas/models/1/{merton_model,merton_report,merton_report_request}.schema.json`, `valuations/schemas/common/1/instrument_pricing_overrides.schema.json` and the attribution, scenarios and statements schemas that embed `AssetDynamics`. UI: `finstack-quant-ui/tests/models/credit/merton.test.tsx` (passes `mean_recovery` and asserts the forced-equality message in `errors.cds_par_spread`), `tests/models/credit/merton.json` and `registry/models/components/merton-calculator/merton-calculator.tsx` (read `implied_equity`).
- **Tests:** add CreditGrades at `ℓ = 30` finite and in `[0,1]` without a clamp; `T = 0` PD equals the closed-form origin mass and is positive; `ℓ = 0` equals driftless flat first passage; `xC < 0` rejected; `debt_spread` for `V/B = 1e6` positive and matching a high-precision reference; `implied_spread` at `pd*lgd = 1e−14` matches `pd*lgd/T` to relative `1e−12`; `cds_par_spread` accepts `R` different from `Lbar`; `implied_equity` rejects first-passage and CreditGrades models; Python `MertonReport.implied_equity` exposes `.value` and `.vol` equal to the WASM object's fields. Re-derive: CreditGrades tests in `calibration.rs` and `dynamics.rs` that pass `mean_recovery`; `default_probability.rs` tests asserting "finite and clamped"; `spreads.rs` test `debt_spread_matches_black_scholes_put_valuation`; `hazard_curve.rs` test `to_hazard_curve_rejects_recovery_inconsistent_with_credit_grades` (deleted with the check) and the `MertonReport` doc sentence in `report.rs` that relies on it; `report.rs` test `a_measure_rejected_for_the_model_keeps_its_error_beside_the_rest` (a first-passage report now records two unavailable measures, not one); `models_parity_golden.json` CreditGrades rows; the UI Merton test above.

  - CREDIT-04 d2 (zero-time and distance rules for terminal and first-passage GBM) and the first-passage half of d3 (reflection combined as `exp(−a²/2)[exp(c²/2)Φ(−c)]`, removal of its NaN-to-zero and clamp): different functions (`pd_from_log_drift`, `first_passage_pd`, `distance_to_default*`); not the audited path.

#### #21 `simulate_portfolio_loss` recovery factor and stream addressing (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `finstack-quant/models/src/correlation/portfolio_loss.rs`: `ValidatedSimulation::new` executes `let _ = uses_recovery_model;` so the documented one-loading requirement is never enforced; `path_loss` passes `systematic = (β·Z)/sqrt(ρ)` as the recovery factor (sign follows the loading) and falls back to `factors[0]` for a zero loading. The same function divides by the norm and hands the copula a `(systematic, rho)` pair, accepts `CopulaSpec::MultiFactor` with two loadings, and draws every role from one `PhiloxRng::with_stream(seed, path_index)` in interleaved order (`PhiloxRng::new(seed, path_index)` after Slice 0 step 0c-1). `validate_exposures` admits a squared loading norm up to `1 + FACTOR_NORM_REL_TOLERANCE` (`64 * f64::EPSILON`, relative). `CreditExposure` and `PortfolioLossConfig` are public-field structs that derive raw `Deserialize` with no `deny_unknown_fields` and no checked constructor. `finstack-quant/models/src/correlation/copula/mod.rs`: `CopulaSpec` derives `Default` (Gaussian), `CopulaSpec::random_factor_loading` clamps `loading_vol` to `[0, 0.5]` and returns `Self`, and deserialization admits an invalid `degrees_of_freedom` or `loading_volatility` that only `build` rejects. No caller exists in `finstack-quant/valuations/**`; structured credit uses `RecoverySpec` directly and never calls this simulator.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Inputs: one horizon and measure for all PDs; literal `u64` seed (zero valid); path count in `1..=1,000,000` (`MAX_PORTFOLIO_LOSS_PATHS` already equals this); confidence in `(0,1)`; each exposure a nonempty already-trimmed unique case-sensitive ID, finite notional `>= 0`, finite PD in `[0,1]`, finite signed loading vector of one common positive dimension. Validate everything, including selected models and stream addresses, before any path. An empty roster with valid controls returns the requested number of `+0` losses with no draws. CREDIT-10 d1.
  2. Severity is one explicit mutually exclusive selection: a required per-name fixed LGD in `[0,1]`, or one common recovery specification; the common-recovery mode takes no per-name LGD. Replaces `CreditExposure.lgd` plus the `Option<&RecoverySpec>` argument. CREDIT-10 d1, d3.
  3. Recovery factor: an explicit factor coordinate is required when the stochastic recovery has nonzero systematic loading and must be in range for a nonempty roster; independent (`a = 0`) and constant recovery take no coordinate. Sample with that coordinate of the unscaled common Gaussian vector `Z` (also under Student-t) and an independent recovery normal per name when needed. Delete the normalized projection and the first-factor fallback. CREDIT-10 d3.
  4. Family: explicit Gaussian or Student-t (`2 < ν <= 1e8`) only; reject `CopulaSpec::MultiFactor` and `RandomFactorLoading` in this route and delete the two-loading `MultiFactor` branch. CREDIT-10 d2.
  5. Latent law: `X_i = β_i·Z + sqrt(1 − Σβ_ij²) ε_i` with signs and coordinates preserved; compare the exact sum of squares with one and reject over-unit vectors with no tolerance, projection or normalization (delete `FACTOR_NORM_REL_TOLERANCE`); zero vector uses only the residual, exact unit vector only the dot product; no divide-by-norm round trip and no flooring of a small positive complement. CREDIT-10 d2.
  6. Default events: literal no-default and certain-default at PD 0 and 1; interior Gaussian defaults at `X_i <= Φ⁻¹(p_i)`; Student-t at `X_i <= t_ν⁻¹(p_i) sqrt(W)` with one `W = χ²_ν/ν` per path from the checked chi-squared inverse on an open uniform, compared in scaled form. CREDIT-10 d2, CREDIT-09 d1, d3.
  7. Streams: Philox keyed by the literal seed; stream ID from the FNV-1a pair function on (identity, role) with roles `portfolio-loss/v1/shared-mixing`, `…/systematic-factor`, `…/idiosyncratic-default`, `…/idiosyncratic-recovery`; identity empty for mixing, ASCII-decimal zero-based factor index for factors, literal exposure ID for name roles. Reserve and check every stream for distinctness before use (every name in both roles, every factor, the mixing slot); a collision fails. In each stream use block `path_index` (uniform slots from `2*path_index`): first open uniform for the mixing inversion, first Box–Muller component of the two uniforms for each normal, companion unused. Open each stream at its block with the existing crate-private `PhiloxRng::with_stream_u01_offset(seed, stream_id, 2*path_index)` and draw through the fallible `next_u01` / `next_std_normal` that Slice 0 step 0c-2 lands; the normal is the reflected transform that 0c-3 lands. CREDIT-10 d6, MC-02 d6 (hash framing), MC-02 d3 (slot addressing and the uniform offset), MC-02 d4 (reflected Box–Muller), MC-02 d5 (fallible draws).
  8. Path sum: checked severity amount per default, summed in ascending UTF-8 byte order of IDs with checked compensated arithmetic; keep exact zeros and `+0`; failures are atomic and report the lowest failing path index; original path order preserved. In scope because steps 3-7 rewrite `path_loss` whole. CREDIT-10 d7.
  9. Hosts: Python and WASM constructors, stubs, exports, parity and wire schemas follow the new exposure/config/severity shapes; exact Python integer seeds, JS safe integer or BigInt, exact raw-JSON integer tokens; lossy JS numbers rejected. CREDIT-10 d10.
  10. Strict restore of the inputs (handed over from Slice 7): `CreditExposure`, `PortfolioLossConfig` and the severity selection become immutable validated state with private fields, checked constructors, `deny_unknown_fields`, and deserialization routed through those constructors, so a restored value passes the same checks as a constructed one. CREDIT-10 d10 ("Build immutable validated state atomically"), CREDIT-10 d1 (validate all supplied data before any path), CN-25 d5 (closed typed objects reject unknown fields).
  11. `CopulaSpec` construction and restore (handed over from Slice 7; in scope because the re-signatured config holds this type as its family selection). Remove the `Default` derive and its Gaussian default (one direct caller, an in-file test; no struct holding a `CopulaSpec` derives `Default`). `CopulaSpec::random_factor_loading` becomes fallible and rejects a `loading_vol` outside finite `[0, 0.5]` instead of clamping (callers: the Python constructor, which becomes `PyResult`; the WASM constructor, already `Result`; four Rust test sites). Route `CopulaSpec` deserialization through the checked constructors so an invalid `degrees_of_freedom` or `loading_volatility` fails at restore, not at `build`; this reaches every payload that embeds a `CopulaSpec` (pool `StochasticDefaultSpec`, tranche and structured-credit configuration). CREDIT-09 d1 (remove the generic Gaussian default and clamping constructors), d4 (`σ` in `[0, .5]`), d11 (validated immutable state on direct and restored inputs).
- **Differs from the old plan:** old plan forced `factor_count == 1` under a recovery model and passed `factors[0]`. Record allows any dimension, requires an explicitly selected recovery coordinate, removes the `MultiFactor` route, and replaces the single interleaved stream with role-addressed streams.

Other Rust sites of step 11: `valuations/credit/tests/cds_tranche/params_tests.rs` (`random_factor_loading`), `models/src/credit/pool/default/spec.rs` (restore). Python `src/bindings/models/correlation/mod.rs`, `finstack_quant/models/correlation/__init__.{py,pyi}`, `finstack-quant-py/README.md` (example). WASM `src/api/models/correlation.rs`, `exports/models/correlation.js`, `tests/wasm_models_correlation.rs` (copula constructors only), `tests/facade/{models_handles,models_parity}.test.mjs`. #### #22 Almgren–Chriss profile calibration and trajectory (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `finstack-quant/models/src/liquidity/almgren_chriss.rs`: `from_profile` sets `delta = 0.5` and `eta = daily_volatility * mid / sqrt(ADV)`; `optimal_trajectory` rejects `|delta − 1| > 1e-12` with a message telling the caller to set `delta` to `1.0`. `finstack-quant/models/src/liquidity/mod.rs`: `almgren_chriss_impact` synthesizes a 20 bp quote around `reference_price.unwrap_or(1.0)` and routes through `from_profile`. `liquidity/impact.rs`: `TradeParams` requires a `profile`, defaults `reference_price` to `profile.mid`, carries `risk_aversion: Option<f64>` defaulted to `1e-6` in the solver, and rejects zero volatility.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Delete `AlmgrenChrissModel::from_profile` and `almgren_chriss_impact` (the profile coefficient recipe and the synthetic 20 bp helper with its unit-price fallback and fixed square-root exponent). Calibration stays with callers. CREDIT-11 d4.
  2. Almgren–Chriss takes explicit finite `γ >= 0`, `η > 0`, `0 < δ <= 1`; `γ` in currency per unit squared, `η` in (currency per unit) per (units per trading day)^`δ`. Document these units on `AlmgrenChrissModel::new`. CREDIT-11 d4.
  3. Execution inputs are explicit: signed finite `Q`, finite `T > 0` trading days, finite `σ_r >= 0` daily decimal, finite arrival price `P_ref > 0`; `σ = P_ref σ_r`. Remove `profile` from `TradeParams`, make `reference_price` required, delete `effective_reference_price`, and admit zero volatility. CREDIT-11 d4.
  4. Almgren–Chriss trajectory requires exact `δ = 1` (delete the `1e-12` tolerance and the misleading hint) and an explicit finite `ρ >= 0` in inverse currency (delete the `1e-6` default); nonlinear `δ` remains available only through the uniform cost estimate. CREDIT-11 d6.
  5. Trajectory: `κ² = ρσ²/η`, `x(t) = Q sinh(κ(T − t))/sinh(κT)`; exact zero `ρ` or `σ` gives the uniform line `Q(T − t)/T` (delete the `|κT| < 1e-12` switch); stable small-argument ratios and scaled exponentials for large arguments. Explicit positive bucket count on the uniform trading-day grid with exact `0, T` and actual adjacent widths `h_j`, built as `TimeGrid::uniform(T, buckets)` (Slice 0 step 0a) in place of `j * dt`; `ExecutionTrajectory::uniform_steps` and `piecewise_linear_variance` in `impact.rs` take the grid's knots and per-bucket widths instead of one `dt`. Signed monotone inventory from `Q` to `+0`; bucket trades are adjacent stored inventory differences. CREDIT-11 d6 (cites CORE-NUMERICS-20 by ID for these knots), CN-20 d4-5, d11. CN-20 d1 describes `TimeGrid` coordinates as model year fractions and leaves the clock to the caller; here the declared clock is trading days.
  6. Score the returned piecewise-linear inventory: permanent cost `γq²/2`, linear temporary cost `η Σ q_j²/h_j`, variance `σ² Σ h_j (a_j² + a_j b_j + b_j²)/3`, with checked sums. Replaces the current `γ |q_j| * average inventory` and `η |rate|^δ |q_j|` loop. CREDIT-11 d6.
  7. Kyle trajectory: uniform schedule with no risk-aversion input, its permanent cost and the same variance rule, no temporary cost. Follows from removing `risk_aversion` from `TradeParams`. CREDIT-11 d6.
  8. Hosts: remove `from_profile` / `fromProfile` and `almgren_chriss_impact` / `almgrenChrissImpact`, re-signature `TradeParams` and both `optimal_trajectory` bindings, register `LiquidityProfile` directly in the schema registry now that `TradeParams` no longer carries it, and update exports, parity, generated schemas, the liquidity lesson and notebook. CREDIT-11 d9.
- **Differs from the old plan:** old plan recalibrated `from_profile` to `δ = 1`, `η = σ_daily * mid / ADV` and kept it as the primary workflow (Confirmed Design Decision 4). Record deletes the recipe and the convenience helper and requires caller-supplied `γ, η, δ, ρ, P_ref`.
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/liquidity/{almgren_chriss,impact,kyle,mod}.rs`, `models/src/schema.rs`, `models/schemas/models/1/trade_params.schema.json` (reshaped), a new `liquidity_profile.schema.json` (step 8), `almgren_chriss_model.schema.json` (field descriptions only), `models/schemas/index.json`. Python `src/bindings/models/liquidity.rs`, `finstack_quant/models/liquidity/__init__.{py,pyi}`, tests `tests/test_models_liquidity.py`, `tests/test_models_wasm_parity.py`, `tests/models/test_senior_review_regressions.py`, `tests/test_run_all_scripts.py`. WASM `src/api/models/liquidity.rs`, `exports/models/liquidity.js`, `tests/wasm_models_liquidity.rs`, `tests/facade/{models_liquidity,models_parity,models_handles}.test.mjs`, `tests/facade/schema_parity.golden.json`. Parity `parity_contract.toml` (`almgren_chriss_impact` in the liquidity public list; `[parity.modules."finstack_quant.models.liquidity"]` `types` map naming `TradeParams`; the `[parity.members."finstack_quant.models.liquidity.TradeParams".renames]` entry for `effective_reference_price`, deleted with the method and its WASM twin `tradeParamsEffectiveReferencePrice`). Docs/examples: `docs-site/content/learn/4/4.4-portfolio-construction-and-liquidity.mdx`, `docs-site/contracts/portfolio-craft.json`, `docs/REFERENCES.md` (entry naming `almgren_chriss_impact`), notebook `05_portfolio/liquidity_risk.ipynb`. Callers in `valuations/**`: none (the `TradeParams` grep hits there are `CDSTradeParams`).
- **Tests:** add: `δ = 1 − 1e-13` rejected; `ρ = 0` and `σ = 0` each give the exact uniform line; large `κT` stays finite and monotone; inventory ends at `+0`; temporary cost equals `η Σ q_j²/h_j` and variance matches the closed form for the uniform line (`σ² Q² T/3`); buy and sell trajectories mirror; Kyle trajectory has zero temporary cost. Delete or re-derive tests in `almgren_chriss.rs` that pin `from_profile` coefficients (`gamma ≈ 5e-7`, `eta ≈ 0.002`), the test accepting `delta = 1.0 − 1e-13`, the two `almgren_chriss_impact` tests in `liquidity/mod.rs`, and the Python/WASM tests listed above.

The bond passes one `coupon_amount` for both today, and BONDS-08 d2 (distinct cash and PIK terms) is deferred. Accept that the Blocker closes as "model is as specified, inputs now explicit", or pull the bond-side derivation of a distinct `P` into this slice?
2. **Seed and stream ownership for the nested comparison.** CREDIT-05 d8 requires an explicit seed and stream per decision and d10 requires exact transfer through pricing overrides; BONDS-08 d6 requires "per-election seed/stream assignment" but gives no formula, and is silent on whether the two antithetic legs share a stream. Proposal: the equity-comparison config carries the seed; the bond supplies the stream as a checked index over (group, leg, coupon parent). Confirm.
3. **#19 jump-diffusion debt spread.** CREDIT-04 d9 wants the jump case to use the bounded mixture rule and report its truncation diagnostic, which is CREDIT-04 d4 (explicit omitted-mass budget and term cap as new inputs). This slice keeps the existing component enumeration for jumps; `debt_spread` will gain inputs and a diagnostic when d4 lands. Accept, or pull d4 in?

First-passage and terminal PD keep their current arithmetic until d2 and the rest of d3 are done. Accept the split?
5. **#21 input layout.** CREDIT-10 d1 and d3 fix the semantics of the severity selection and recovery coordinate but not the type layout (LGD list aligned to exposures versus per-exposure field inside a fixed-LGD variant). Proposal: a severity enum on the config with `FixedLgd` holding one value per exposure in roster order and `CommonRecovery { recovery, factor_index }`. Confirm.
6. **#21 `MultiFactor` rename.** CREDIT-09 d6 renames the shared `CopulaSpec::MultiFactor` to `TwoFactorGaussian` with an explicit sector share. Step 11 already edits `CopulaSpec`, but the rename reaches the tranche and pool consumers and both hosts (`multi_factor` / `multiFactor`, `is_multi_factor`). Keep it deferred with the kernels, or pull the rename in so `CopulaSpec` is re-signatured once?

CREDIT-04 d12 replaces that map with typed unavailable reasons and removes the default point and grades. Accept the alignment now and the report reshape later, or pull the d12 report decisions into #19?

#### #12 PDE exercise, damping, Greeks, Heston bridge (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `PenaltyExercise::apply` in `finstack-quant/models/src/pde/exercise.rs` runs after the linear solve and blends `u/(1+P) + P·g/(1+P)`, so a projected node stays below its payoff; exercise times match with `1e-10` there and in `Solver1D::solve` (`pde/solver.rs`). `BlackScholesPde` (`pde/bridge.rs`) and `HestonPde` (`pde/bridge2d.rs`) are unvalidated `pub`-field structs, and `HestonPde` substitutes `y.max(0.0)` for variance. `PdeSolution::{interpolate,delta,gamma}` clamp, snap to the nearest node and return zero on tiny widths. The equity owner `valuations/equity/src/equity_option/pde_pricer.rs` returns the bare grid interpolant with no actual-spot exercise comparison.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Replace `PenaltyExercise` with an explicit projection type (record does not name it; pick one short name). Delete `penalty_factor`, `iterations`, the `dt` argument of `apply`, and `ExerciseError::{InvalidPenalty, InvalidTimeStep}`. Fields become private. — 015 d1, d5
  2. Projection rule per interior node: take `G_i` when `G_i > C_i`, else keep `C_i`; ties keep continuation; copy the selected ordinate. Validate the whole continuation vector before writing any node. — 015 d5
  3. Keep one fixed payoff vector on the interior knots, validated for shape and finiteness at build; signed entries stay valid. — 015 d2
  4. Exercise set: American compares at every receiving knot in `[0,T)`. Bermudan requires a nonempty set of distinct exact knots in `[0,T)`; reject nonfinite, out-of-horizon, missing and repeated times, and reject `T` itself (currently accepted). Remove both `1e-10` matches; membership is `TimeGrid::get_index` on the prepared grid from Slice 0 step 0a, one returned index per exercise time. — 015 d3-4, 014 d2, CORE-NUMERICS-20 d8
  5. Build the 1D time grid from the core `TimeGrid` canonical knots (`T·(i/N)`, exact 0 and T) traversed in reverse, with actual widths `h = t_old − t_new`; let the builder accept a uniform count or a prepared grid/exact union with required exercise times. Delete `backward_time_levels` (clamped `T − i·dt`); `McsStepper::time_levels` shares it and moves to the same canonical uniform grid. This follows Slice 0 step 0a, which lands the canonical `uniform(t_max, num_steps)` knots, `uniform_with_required_times(t_max, num_steps, required_times)` as an exact union with no drop or snap, `get_times`/`get_dts` and `get_index` in `core/src/math/time_grid.rs`; this slice consumes them and adds no time-grid arithmetic of its own (the PDE module does not use `TimeGrid` today). — 014 d1-2, 016 d1, CORE-NUMERICS-20 d4-6, d8
  6. Rename the 1D startup: `ThetaStepper::rannacher` and `Solver1DBuilder::rannacher` become damped Crank–Nicolson with explicit `K`; require `0 ≤ K ≤ N` at admission (unchecked today). Keep `K = 4` for the equity route and `K = 2` for the barrier route. — 014 d5
  7. `Solver1D` applies exercise after each eligible incoming step and never resets the damping count. A solver failure is never replaced by the payoff. Both already hold in `Solver1D::solve`; keep. — 015 d6
  8. Replace `PdeSolution.exercise_boundary: Option<Vec<(f64, f64)>>` with adjacent-node brackets: receiving time (the stored knot), both knot endpoints, and the side that strictly prefers exercise, computed from unprojected values with strict `G > C`. Ties belong to the continuation side (today `u > payoff` puts ties on the exercise side). Backward time order, increasing spatial order. — 015 d7
  9. Reconstruct the two spatial edges from the projected final interior at the output time; edges get no obstacle comparison. Already so in `Solver1D::solve`; keep. Reject Linear/LinearInExp without two interior nodes at admission, and remove the constant-extension fallback in `boundary_value` (`solver.rs`) together with the matching `else` branches of the Linear/LinearInExp elimination in `operator.rs` (the `upper.len() > 1` test at the lower edge and the `last > 0` test at the upper edge), so elimination and reconstruction keep one relation. — 015 d8, 013 d1, d6, d10
  10. `PdeSolution` and `PdeSolution2D`: private fields with read-only accessors; `interpolate`, `delta`/`gamma`, `delta_x`/`gamma_x` become fallible and reject NaN, infinities and any query outside the closed solved domain. Remove endpoint clamping (in `Grid1D::interpolate`, and the independent x and y clamping in `Grid2D::interpolate`, `grid2d.rs`). The barrier pricer's interior-only extraction through `Grid1D::interpolate` takes the same shape and finiteness checks. — 011 d5-7
  11. Coordinate derivatives: compute the local-quadratic nodal estimates (interior triple, first/last triple at the ends) and interpolate them at the query (tensor interpolation in 2D). Remove `find_nearest` snapping and the `1e-30` zero branches in `delta_1d`/`gamma_1d`. Document that results are derivatives in `x`. — 011 d8-10
  12. No spot-Greek helper on the generic solution. If an owner elects spot Greeks from `x = log S`, it uses `Δ = U_x/S`, `Γ = (U_xx − U_x)/S²` at the actual spot; live product Greeks stay on bump-and-reprice. — 011 d11, 015 d10
  13. Equity owner (`pde_pricer.rs`): after the checked grid value, when the valuation phase allows immediate exercise, take the greater of that value and the literal payoff at the actual spot, before quantity scaling and Money. Build nodal exercise payoffs from the bridge's checked payoff expression rather than a second inline copy. — 015 d9, 017 d6
  14. `BlackScholesPde`: private fields and a checked constructor admitting explicit call/put, finite positive strike, finite signed `r` and `q`, finite `σ ≥ 0`. — 017 d1
  15. `HestonPde`: private fields, built from the checked `HestonPricingParams` wrapper (finite signed `r`, `q` plus the validated `HestonParams` block) with positive strike and side. Domain is `v0 ≥ 0`, `κ > 0`, `θ_v > 0`, `σ_v ≥ 0`, `−1 < ρ < 1`; Feller violation is admitted. Remove every `y.max(0.0)` (four coefficients); require each variance coordinate finite and nonnegative and normalize a represented zero. This follows Slice 0 step 0d (immutable `HestonParams`, `HestonPricingParams` built from the two rates and the checked model, read-only accessors; MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5-7) and Slice 3 #13b (the classical domain change to `v0 ≥ 0`, `σ_v ≥ 0`; MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4). This slice consumes that type and does not change `HestonParams::new`. — 017 d2, d4, MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d4-6
  16. Variance axis: starts at exact zero, upper bound above the actual `v0`, enough interior nodes for Linear at both edges; query admitted at `v0 = 0`. Keep `BoundaryCondition::Linear` at both variance edges and correct the module doc that says the v-lower edge "reduces to" the 1D PDE: Linear is a local numerical closure that neither integrates the limiting boundary PDE nor makes zero absorbing. In the owner (`pde2d_pricer.rs`) the axis already starts at exact 0 and the `v0` range check already admits 0; remove the `v0 > 0` half of the PDE owner admission that Slice 3 #13b adds under MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d20, and confirm the variance grid still builds when its sinh centre `theta_v.min(v0)` is the lower edge. Must land with step 15: the wide domain is not admitted at the PDE without it. — 017 d8, 013 d6
  17. Zero vol-of-vol: at literal `σ_v = 0` the European Heston PDE owner returns the checked discounted Black price with total standard deviation `sqrt(I)`, `I = T[v0·A(κT) + θ_v·(1 − A(κT))]`, `A(z) = (1 − e^{−z})/z`, the small complementary weight evaluated directly; it does not march the ADI grid, whose variance diffusion and mixed term vanish there. Every positive `σ_v` uses the stochastic PDE, including `v0 = 0`; zero is an explicit parameter case with no threshold, and a failed grid or stochastic solve propagates instead of falling back to Black. Remove the `σ_v > 0` half of the same owner admission. `I` and the Black branch are the ones Slice 3 #13b lands (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d10, MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d5); use them, do not write a second copy in `valuations/equity`. Record silent on how the owner reaches them; simplest consistent choice: call the models Heston price entry, which returns exactly this value at literal zero (see open questions). Must land with step 15 for the same reason as step 16. — 017 d13, MODELS-ANALYTIC-09 d5
- **Differs from the old plan:** Exact max projection matches. The record contradicts five fixes the audit and old plan asked for, so they are dropped: implicit Forsyth–Vetzal penalty (explicit projection is the method, 015 d1); half-step Rannacher in 1D (full-interval startup, no halfsteps, 014 d5; halfsteps exist only in the 2D startup, 016 d6, already coded); restart after Bermudan exercise (015 d6); a Fichera row at v = 0 (Linear closure kept, 017 d8); `spot_delta`/`spot_gamma` on `PdeSolution` (coordinate units kept, 011 d11; no valuations caller reads `delta`/`gamma`). The audit's CFL formula repair is also dropped because 014 d3 deletes θ < ½ and the check (Deferred). The Heston domain is `κ > 0, θ_v > 0, σ_v ≥ 0`, not `κ ≥ 0, θ ≥ 0, σ_v > 0`, with `v0 = 0` priced on the Linear closure and `σ_v = 0` routed to the exact Black branch (017 d8, d13); Black–Scholes admits `σ ≥ 0`.

#### #5 `BinomialTree` discrete cash dividends (Blocker)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `BinomialTree::price_with_discrete_dividends` in `finstack-quant/models/src/trees/binomial_tree.rs` snaps each dividend to `round().clamp(1, N)`, so a late dividend lands on step `N`, is removed from the escrowed spot at the root, and never meets a cum exercise test because `price_american` exercises on `0..N` and `value_at_maturity` uses the ex spot only. The root cause is the snap, which the record forbids. The audit's second claim (reserve should be discounted at `r`) is contradicted: the record carries the reserve at `b = r − q`, which is what the model code does. On the live equity path the owner (`early_exercise_market_params` in `pricing/inputs.rs`) passes `dividend_yield = 0` whenever future cash dividends exist, so the reserve is in effect carried at `r` there today; that is the owner's schedule-driven yield suppression (002 d16), not the model's carry.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Keep the reserve at `b = r − q`; do not change it to `r`. — 002 d6, d10
  2. Remove the snap. Each live cash jump and each explicit exercise opportunity must sit exactly on a lattice node; an off-grid event, a failed clock conversion or two distinct boundaries collapsing to one node is an error naming the event, clock and grid. No clamp, omission, reorder, dedup or hidden step. The equity record accepts the consequence: nearest nodes, parity adjustment and implicit tree replacement are removed, and a halfway event cannot sit on an odd LR tree. — 002 d7-8, VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d4
  3. Re-signature the model entry to take a prepared contract instead of `&[(f64, f64)]` and `&[f64]` times: jumps `J` by node index, the post-expiry reserve through horizon `H ≥ T`, the origin phase (cum or ex), the terminal payoff phase, and the eligible `(node, phase)` exercise opportunities. The models crate does no time-to-node mapping. — 002 d5, d7, d9, d13, d15
  4. Delete `map_exercise_dates_to_steps` from `finstack-quant/core/src/math/time_grid.rs` together with its tests; `binomial_tree.rs` is its only non-test caller. Also drop the `sort`/`dedup` in `price_bermudan`. — CORE-NUMERICS-20 d12, 002 d8
  5. Reserve and spot: `R_ex(t) = Σ_{e>t} J_e·exp(−b·(e−t))`, `R_cum(t) = R_ex(t) + J_t`, using each event's canonical node label; `X0 = S0 − R_origin(0) > 0`; `S = X + R` per phase. Remove the `(dividend_time − node_time).max(0.0)` repair. — 002 d10
  6. Terminal: one payoff at the owner-selected cum or ex phase. No automatic `max` of both. A cum exercise before an ex terminal payoff exists only if the owner supplied that right as its own opportunity. — 002 d9, d13
  7. Interior cash node: continuation on the ex side; compare with ex intrinsic only if ex exercise is eligible; carry through the jump; compare with cum intrinsic only if cum exercise is eligible. Replace the unconditional `continuation.max(ex).max(cum)` in `value_at_node`. American includes the root only when exercisable. — 002 d12-13
  8. Leisen–Reimer centering uses `K_X = K − R_terminal` and requires `K_X > 0`; all exercise comparisons keep the original `K` on full spot. Today LR is centered on `K`. — 002 d11
  9. `BinomialTree::new` validates the count at construction and no longer bumps an even LR count to odd; LR with an even count is an error. Needed here because exact placement must be proven on the count actually used. Defaults are odd (201 for equity, commodity and futures risk; 401 for futures PV), but a caller-supplied even `tree_steps` on an LR path now errors, and the `tree_steps` doc in `valuations/core/src/instruments/pricing_overrides.rs` uses 100 as its example: fix the doc and regenerate the schema. — 001 d4, d25, VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d4
  10. Equity owner (`valuations/equity/src/equity_option/pricing/{black.rs, inputs.rs}`): prepare the contract once from dates, proving node placement on the declared clock (for ACT/365F, `N·elapsed_days = j·expiry_days`), supply origin and terminal phase and exercise eligibility explicitly, and reuse the same prepared inputs for price and tree Greeks. Every bump in `tree_finite_difference_greeks` reprices on the same selected tree and prepared event geometry (VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d5; this is the reading chosen here for the theta reprice, pending open question 7, since 002 d16 asks valuation rolls to re-prepare from dates), so `price_fn` passes the one node-indexed contract; delete `remaining_dividends` and the shifted-time Bermudan filter (`time − elapsed`), which would move every event off its node in the theta reprice, and the `dropped_divs` spot adjustment with its `.max(1e-8)` floor (see open questions for the theta consequence). The existing wire carries no phase, so the owner states its current convention in code as explicit inputs: ex-phase terminal payoff, no separate cum right at a coincident expiry/ex boundary, both phases eligible at interior American cash nodes, `J = D` under the ex-date-only schedule and `H = T`. Wire fields for phase and pay date come with the deferred 002 d2-4 work. — 002 d7, d9, d15-16, VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d4-5
  11. Commodity and futures callers (`valuations/exotics/src/commodity/commodity_spot_option/types.rs`, `valuations/core/src/instruments/common_impl/listed/future_option/types.rs`): pass no cash, supply exact Bermudan node opportunities, and propagate placement failures. — 002 d16, 001 d24
- **Differs from the old plan:** The old plan changed escrow discounting to `r` and took an automatic `max(ex, cum)` at the terminal step. The record keeps `r − q`, makes terminal phase and the cum right explicit owner inputs, and removes the snap that created the defect.
- **Files:**
  - Rust: `finstack-quant/models/src/trees/binomial_tree.rs`, `finstack-quant/core/src/math/time_grid.rs`
  - Callers: `valuations/equity/src/equity_option/pricing/{black.rs, inputs.rs, mod.rs}`, `valuations/exotics/src/commodity/commodity_spot_option/types.rs`, `valuations/core/src/instruments/common_impl/listed/future_option/types.rs`, `models/tests/production_equity_audit.rs`, `valuations/tests/instruments/equity_option/test_finance_regressions.rs`, `finstack-quant-wasm/tests/facade/valuations_audit_regressions.test.mjs`, `models/tests/fixtures/production_lr_quantlib.json` with `scripts/golden/lr_quantlib_reference.py`
  - Schema: the `tree_steps` description is generated text; `mise run gen-write` rewrites it in `valuations/schemas/common/1/instrument_pricing_overrides.schema.json`, the statements, attribution and scenarios schemas that embed it, 4 files under `finstack-quant-wasm/types/generated/` and 228 files under `finstack-quant-ui/src/generated/`. No `.pyi` carries it.
  - Python / WASM / parity: no API change (`BinomialTree` has no binding; `EquityOption.discrete_dividends` wire shape unchanged; placement and parity errors propagate through existing pricing routes)
- **Tests:**
  - `cash_dividend_at_maturity_is_ex_dividend_at_expiry` pins the ex phase implicitly; restate it with an explicit ex terminal phase and add cum-terminal and "cum right before ex terminal" cases.
  - `cash_dividend_tree_preserves_continuous_carry_in_deterministic_limit` and `vanishing_cash_dividend_converges_to_continuous_yield_price` place a dividend at `T/2` on a 101-step LR tree; under 002 d8 that is an error. Split into CRR pass and LR rejection.
  - `test_exercise_schedule_mapping` goes with the deleted helper, as does the core test of `map_exercise_dates_to_steps` in `time_grid.rs`.
  - `test_leisen_reimer_helper` asserts the even-to-odd bump (100 to 101, 200 to 201) and becomes a rejection test; `test_bermudan_between_european_and_american` and `test_golden_lr_odd_converges_faster` build LR with 100 steps and need an odd count, and the first also needs its quarterly dates on nodes, which no odd count allows, so it moves to CRR with a multiple of 4.

None of the three early dates can sit on a node of those trees, so `models/tests/production_equity_audit.rs` and the WASM facade test `finstack-quant-wasm/tests/facade/valuations_audit_regressions.test.mjs` ("equity LR prices ... QuantLib cases") reject them under 002 d7-8. Regenerate the Bermudan rows from `scripts/golden/lr_quantlib_reference.py` on a compatible schedule and count, or drop them; day 365 is expiry, not an early right.
  - `tree_theta_rolls_expiry_dividends_and_bermudan_dates` in `valuations/tests/instruments/equity_option/test_finance_regressions.rs` pins the rolled-time theta that step 10 removes; restate it once open question 7 is settled.
  - Product-level cases that put a dated cash dividend or a Bermudan date on the default 201-step (or a 51-step) LR tree reject instead of moving, unless rebuilt on a compatible grid. Check each for node alignment before re-deriving anything: the inline tests in `valuations/equity/src/equity_option/pricing/mod.rs` (for example `american_discrete_dividend_preserves_pre_exercise_spot`), the cash-dividend American cases in `valuations/tests/instruments/equity_option/test_finance_regressions.rs`, `test_spot_bermudan_prices_between_european_and_american` in `valuations/exotics/tests/commodity_spot_option/pricing.rs` and the style-parametrised dividend case in `finstack-quant-py/tests/test_valuations_credit_equity_fx.py`.
  - `binomial_tree_prices_are_bit_pinned` and equity goldens with cash dividends on LR: re-derive (new `K_X` centering, no even-to-odd bump, exact node labels).

#### #6 `RatesCreditTree` calibration and node state (Blocker)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `solve_floored_theta` in `finstack-quant/models/src/trees/two_factor_rates_credit/calibration.rs` returns the all-floored shift as success when the target is unreachable (counting `unreachable_steps`), returns `0.0` on a nonpositive target or mass, and falls back to `0.5·(lo+hi)` when Brent construction or the solve fails (`BrentSolver::new(1e-12, 100)` with hard-coded controls, after a 200-round doubling loop for the upper bracket); the rate fit has its own `else { 0.0 }`. `calibrate` performs no replay. `pricing.rs` passes `interest_rate: Some(r_t.max(1e-8))` at terminal and induction nodes while discounting at the signed rate.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Targets: replace `RatesCreditCalibrationTargets.survival_probabilities` with local integrated hazards `H[0..N−1]`, finite and nonnegative; require `D[0] = 1` and positive finite later `D`. `build_rates_credit_targets` and `build_daily_bond_rates_credit_targets` (`valuations/core/src/instruments/pricing/rates_credit.rs`) capture `H` from the hazard source's native local evaluation (`HazardCurve::integrated_hazard_between_dates` exists in `core/src/market_data/term_structures/hazard_curve/curve.rs`) on the dated node boundaries, and stop preparing targets from ratios and log-interpolation of rounded survivals. `bond/pricing/hazard.rs` reads `targets.survival_probabilities` at three sites and moves to `H` with it. — 008 d3-4
  2. Rate fit: `alpha = (log(Σ_j Q_j·exp(−h·x_j)) − log D[n+1]) / h` on the actual incoming discount mass, root and deterministic rows by the same identity; a nonpositive mass or target is an error, not a zero shift. Keep the short rate signed. — 008 d9
  3. Hazard law: top raw intensity `b`, raw hazard `b − d_j`, effective `λ_j = max(b − d_j, 0)` (the existing `effective_hazard`). Fit against normalized actual survival state prices `w_j = q_j/W`: `−log(Σ_j w_j·exp(−h·λ_j)) = H[n]`; never substitute the previous quoted survival for `W`. — 008 d10-11
  4. `H[n] = 0`: set `b = 0` with no solve. Positive `H[n]` on one node: `b = H[n]/h`. — 008 d12
  5. Positive `H[n]` on several nodes: bracket `[A, A + d_max]` with `A = H[n]/h`; tighten the upper end to `−log1p(−L/w_top)/h` when `L = −expm1(−H[n]) < w_top` and that is smaller. Solve with the checked Brent that d13 delegates to. It is already landed in `finstack-quant/core/src/math/solver.rs` (`BrentSolver::new(tolerance, max_iterations) -> Result`, private controls, `solve_in_bracket` with a fallible callback, endpoints sampled lower then upper with a computed zero accepted) and this function already calls it, so no solver work is needed: pass the proven bracket, a fallible objective and the explicit controls, and propagate the result with `?`. An endpoint may meet the gate. Failed objective, bracket or solve returns an error. Delete the all-floored success return, the `return 0.0` guard, the doubling loop and `unwrap_or(midpoint)`. — 008 d13, CORE-NUMERICS-05 d2-3, d5, d9
  6. Use `expm1`/`log1p` for the local default complement and log survival. — 008 d14
  7. Replay before publishing the candidate: discount relative error in bp against one explicit finite positive tolerance; one explicit survival tolerance in `(0, 10000)` relative bp applied to local positive `H` error and to cumulative survival error from the log ratio; zero-loss intervals stay exactly zero. Report worst errors and earliest intervals. The existing atomic candidate swap stays. — 008 d15, d18
  8. Reshape `HazardFloorSaturation`: remove `unreachable_steps`; report zero-effective-hazard mass and strictly negative raw share separately, normalized by incoming mass, root included, terminal excluded; apply an explicit inclusive maximum zero-hazard share in `[0,1]` on positive-volatility rows with positive `H`. — 008 d16
  9. The two tolerances, the floor-share limit and the Brent controls (coordinate tolerance in units of `b` and `max_iterations`, replacing the hard-coded `1e-12` and `100`) are explicit inputs on `RatesCreditConfig`, supplied by `resolve_rates_credit_config` (`valuations/core/src/instruments/pricing_overrides.rs`) as product-owned values. Record silent on the numbers; see open questions. — 008 d13, d15-16, d19
  10. Node state: departing nodes get the signed `Some(r_t)`; delete both `.max(1e-8)`. The terminal node has no outgoing interval, so its `NodeState` carries no `interest_rate` or `hazard_rate` (neither `value_at_maturity` in `bond_valuator.rs` nor `term_loan/pricing/tree_engine.rs` reads them). — 008 d9, d18, 003 d11
  11. Credit callbacks that need the applied discount factor treat a missing `state.df` as an error; remove the `interest_rate.unwrap_or(0.0)` fallback in `bond_valuator.rs` and `term_loan/pricing/tree_engine.rs`. Only the rates-credit lattice sets `hazard_rate` in production; unit tests in those two files build such states by hand and must supply `df`. — 003 d10
- **Differs from the old plan:** Same intent on zero shift only at `H = 0`, propagated errors, no midpoint and replay of both marginals. The record adds a re-typed target (`H`, not survival), the proven bracket, replay tolerances in bp as explicit inputs instead of a hard-coded `1e-6`, a reshaped floor diagnostic with a gate, and no rate at the terminal node (the old plan passed `Some(r_t)` there).
- **Files:**
  - Rust: `finstack-quant/models/src/trees/two_factor_rates_credit/{mod.rs, calibration.rs, pricing.rs, tests.rs}`
  - Callers: `valuations/core/src/instruments/pricing/rates_credit.rs`, `valuations/core/src/instruments/pricing_overrides.rs`, `valuations/fixed-income/src/bond/pricing/hazard.rs`, `valuations/fixed-income/src/bond/pricing/tree/{tree_pricer.rs, bond_valuator.rs, tests.rs, lsmc/tests.rs}`, `valuations/fixed-income/src/term_loan/pricing/tree_engine.rs`, `valuations/tests/instruments/bond/metrics/callable_credit_risk.rs`, `models/tests/rates_credit_tree.rs`. The `lsmc/{mod.rs, build.rs, replay.rs, state.rs}` files only read a calibrated tree and should need no change.
  - Python / WASM / parity / schema: none if the new controls are product constants; `instrument_pricing_overrides.schema.json`, `.pyi` and WASM typed overrides change only if the owner wants them on the wire
- **Tests:**
  - `hazard_floor_saturation_reports_binding_floor` pins the old struct and "saturated but accepted" behaviour; restate against the new shares and the gate.
  - New: unreachable target errors; `H = 0` row exact; single-node row; bracket endpoints; Brent failure propagates; replay failure leaves the prior calibration intact; negative-rate lattice passes signed rates to a recording valuator; terminal state has no rate or hazard.
  - Every test that builds `RatesCreditCalibrationTargets` from survival probabilities is re-derived with `H`; callable-bond and term-loan goldens on the rates-credit path can move at solver-tolerance level.
- **Deferred:**
  - 008 d1-2 (factor definition, prepared uniform grid with integer day-count proof): grid construction is untouched.
  - 008 d5-8 (centered exact-OU binary law, support and variance-retention admission, one-state deterministic factor, prepare-once transitions): this replaces `mean_reverting_up_prob` and with it removes `KAPPA_MAX` (also in `pricing_overrides.rs`, `bond/types/pricing.rs` and schema text), the `sigma.max(1e-12)` floor and the `.clamp(0, 1)`. It is separable: steps 2-8 are written against stored node values and branch weights and the bracket proof needs only nonnegative weights and monotone effective hazard, so nothing built here is deleted by it.
  - 008 d17, d19-20: variance-of-floor diagnostic, product reconciliation and qualification.
  - Removing the stored synthetic terminal row and the sampled terminal interval substitute: 009 d12, sampling path.
  - Rest of 003 (only d1 and d10-11 were read): OAS basis and the general rollback contract are not on this finding's path.

#### Open questions for the owner

1. **CORE-NUMERICS-20 is Slice 0 step 0a; this slice follows it.** Resolved for #12: steps 4 and 5 consume the canonical knots, actual widths, exact required-time union and `get_index` that 0a lands (d4-6, d8). The deletion of `map_exercise_dates_to_steps` (d12) stays here in #5 step 4, as 0a states. `map_date_to_step` (convertible valuator) is left in core for its owner; Slice 0 carries that question.
2. **`HestonParams` domain: resolved, with one call-shape question.** The type reshaping (MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d5-7) is Slice 0 step 0d and the classical domain change with its Fourier branches is Slice 3 #13b; this slice follows both and lands the two PDE branches with the admission (017 d8 in step 16, 017 d13 in step 17), removing the PDE owner gate #13b adds. Still open: MODELS-ANALYTIC-08 d10 keeps the integrated-variance arithmetic private to `models`, so the equity owner in step 17 cannot compute `I` itself. Confirm it should call the models Heston price entry at literal `σ_v = 0`, or name the checked function #13b should expose.
3. **Dividend placement will reject real schedules.** Accepted by 002 d7-8 and VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d4, but worth confirming before it ships: with the default 201-step LR tree, almost no ex-date lands on a node, so American and Bermudan equity options with future cash dividends will error until the owner selects a compatible grid. The same holds without dividends for Bermudan exercise dates, on both the equity and the commodity route, which snap today. Is deriving the step count from the day counts wanted in this slice?
4. **Phase inputs without a wire field.** Step 10 of #5 hard-codes the current phase convention in the equity owner because `discrete_dividends` is `Vec<(Date, f64)>`. Is that acceptable until 002 d2-4 change the wire, or should the phase and cum-right fields (schema, `typed_equity` bindings, `.pyi`, parity) be pulled into this slice?
5. **Replay and floor controls.** 008 d15-16 require explicit owner values (discount bp tolerance, survival relative-bp tolerance, maximum zero-hazard share) and d13 explicit Brent controls. What values, and are they product constants or pricing-override fields (which adds schema and binding work)?
6. **Size of the Greeks item.** 011 d5-10 make every `PdeSolution` and `PdeSolution2D` query fallible and replace the derivative algorithm, though no product reads those derivatives. Keep it here, or limit this slice to d5-7 (needed by 015 d9) and move d8-10 out?
7. **Tree theta under exact placement.** Today the one-day theta reprice shortens `T` and shifts every dividend and exercise time by a day, which cannot sit on nodes of the shortened grid. Step 10 of #5 reads VALUATIONS-EQUITY-AND-COMPOSITION-07 d5 ("the same selected tree and prepared event geometry", local model time sensitivity kept distinct from the dated cash-inclusive horizon calculation) as: reprice the same node-indexed contract at the shorter `T`. Confirm that reading, or say that theta should re-prepare from dates under 002 d16 and fail when the rolled grid is inadmissible.
8. **Yield suppression.** The equity owner sets `q = 0` whenever future cash dividends exist. 002 d16 removes that and this slice defers it, although step 10 rewrites the same function and the affected goldens are re-derived here. Pull the removal into step 10 so they move once, or keep it deferred?

#### #10 HW1F issuer LSMC sparse fallback; `Hw1fMcPricer` discards pricing groups (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** `finstack-quant/models/src/rates/hw1f/lsmc.rs`: when `active_paths.len() <= num_basis + 2` the else-branch compares each path's call amount with its own realized `post_at_ex` and exercises on it. Unlike the audit says, it fires in BOTH `oos_lsmc` modes and is applied to every path, including held-out pricing paths. Rows with `exercise_value <= 0.0` are dropped from the fit and the decision. `ShortRateBasis` (`hw1f/exercise.rs`) is `[1, r, r^2, t*r]` (degree < 3) or six terms; at a fixed date `t*r` is collinear with `r`, hidden only by the truncating SVD. `hw1f/mc.rs`: there is no `valuation_offset`; `Hw1fMcPricer::price` passes `config.split()` to `money_estimate_from_pairs`, which drops the even-stream half when `oos_lsmc` is set. `r0` is unchecked in both pricers.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. Remove the realized-future fallback and the `+ 2` threshold. For `p` fitted columns require at least `max(2,p)` training base groups with eligible observations, sufficient rows and full numerical rank; otherwise ERROR with the decision date and actual coverage. Zero eligible rows is also an error. Consequence to accept: callable range accrual now errors where it priced off realized cash; its owner must exclude structurally unavailable decisions from the schedule. MA-26 d18
  2. Eligibility comes from live status at the decision, not from the call amount: remove both `exercise_value <= 0.0` skips; zero or signed call amounts are valid rows. Each eligible training leg is one equally weighted row; each base group counts once for the gate. MA-26 d3 (amount clause), d20
  3. Univariate short-rate basis: literal degree 0 through 3, features `1, z, ..., z^degree`, one fit per date; delete `t*z` and `t*z^2`, the `>= 3` bucketing and the implicit degree default. Inseparable from step 1: the current rows are rank-deficient by construction. MA-26 d14
  4. Degree 0 (intercept-only, checked training mean) only when the caller DECLARES the decision state structurally common; never inferred from identical rows, never substituted for a sparse fit. MA-26 d15
  5. Fit with the strict scaled full-rank SVD of #11 step 5 (one solver, same explicit `tau` and iteration limit). MA-26 d16, d17
  6. Replace parity splitting and in-sample pricing on this route by explicit, separately supplied positive training and pricing base counts: training uses base IDs `[0, N_train)`, pricing the following `N_price`; at least two pricing groups; antithetic legs stay in their group; only pricing groups enter the mean and 95% interval. The new control structs carry no registry `Default`, no `paths.max(1)` floor. MA-26 d19, d23 (pricing-statistics clause); MA-25 d17 (defaults clause, for the new structs)
  7. Call rule stays strict `K_i < C_hat_i`, identical in rollback and frozen replay on pricing groups. MA-26 d10, d22 (replay clause)
  8. Delete the module docs asserting an in-sample-upper / held-out-lower bracket. MA-26 d25
  9. Plain `Hw1fMcPricer` uses every completed base group and requires at least two: its controls are base-group count, seed and antithetic, plus `min_steps_between_events`, which stays until MA-25 d9 lands (see Deferred); remove `basis_degree`, `oos_lsmc` and `SampleSplit` from the plain driver. MA-25 d17 (exercise-fields clause), d22
  10. Do NOT add an `r0.is_finite()` check: the record removes the separate initial-rate input (open question 5). MA-25 d1
- **Differs from the old plan:** Old plan substituted a cross-sectional mean (or lower-degree fit) on sparse dates and validated `r0`. The record fails sparse live fits, allows an intercept only for a declared structurally common state, drops the `t`-terms, replaces the parity split by explicit disjoint counts, and removes `r0` rather than validating it.
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/rates/hw1f/{lsmc.rs,mc.rs,config.rs,exercise.rs,mod.rs}`, `models/src/monte_carlo/pricer/lsq.rs`. Issuer-route callers: `valuations/exotics/src/exotics/callable_range_accrual/pricer.rs`, `valuations/exotics/src/exotics/hw1f_note.rs`, `valuations/exotics/tests/exotic_harness/bermudan_swaption_parity.rs`. Plain-route callers: `valuations/exotics/src/exotics/{snowball,tarn}/pricer.rs`, `valuations/rates/src/cap_floor/hw_pricer.rs`, `valuations/exotics/tests/exotic_harness/{european_option_analytical,tarn_tree_parity,hw1f_curve_repricing}.rs`, `valuations/benches/{rate_exotics_pricing,mc_pricing}.rs`. `Hw1fMcConfig {` struct literals: 69 in 21 files, including holder routes that still read `oos_lsmc`/`basis_degree`/`split()`: `valuations/rates/src/swaption/pricing/{bermudan.rs,lmm.rs,monte_carlo_lsmc.rs,lmm_bermudan.rs,tests/numeraire.rs}`, `valuations/rates/tests/{performance/bermudan.rs,performance/lmm_consumer.rs,swaption/bermudan_pricing.rs}` (open question 4); `calibration/src/lmm.rs` has two literals in inline tests that set only `num_paths` and `min_steps_between_events`. The `rust.hw1f` registry block in `models/src/monte_carlo/registry.rs` validates `basis_degree` as positive, so degree 0 is unreachable from the registry. Python / WASM / parity / schema: `Hw1fMcConfig` is not bound and has no schema; host change only if the callable-range-accrual override surface gains training/pricing counts (MA-26 d27, open question 4).
- **Tests:** sparse date errors with date and coverage; zero-amount call rows included; degree-2 design at one date has rank 3; training outcomes never enter the reported mean; plain MC with `N` groups reports `N` estimators. Pinned old behaviour to re-derive: `hw1f/config.rs` tests `split_partitions_by_stream_parity` and `default_values`; the `oos_lsmc` loops in `hw1f/mc.rs` tests; `callable_range_accrual/pricer.rs` tests and goldens (basis, eligibility and sample layout all move); `bermudan_swaption_parity.rs` (`ShortRateBasis::new(2).row`).
- **Deferred:** MA-26 d1, d2, d4, d5 (owner economics, decision mapping, settlement, no-call route; d2 includes replacing the `1e-12` event matching in `hw1f/{mc,lsmc}.rs` by CN-20 `get_index`, which Slice 0 0a leaves to this record: it is a lookup change independent of the fit and goes with MA-25 d9, d10); d6-d9 (dated branch cash, removal of default `value_after`: a cash-representation redesign the fit consumes unchanged); d11, d12; d13, d26 (accrued in-range state in the basis: callable-range owner); d21 (already the structure); d23 remaining diagnostics, d24, d27, d28. MA-25 d1-d8 (centered state, exact joint log-bank step replacing trapezoid `bank_step_factor`); d9 (remove `min_steps_between_events` and the monthly subdivision in `build_event_aligned_grid`: not unblocked by Slice 0 0a alone, because the `O(dt^2)` error of the trapezoid `bank_step_factor` is controlled by those sub-steps until the exact joint step of d2-d7 replaces it; the field stays on the plain controls until then, a deletion only); d10-d16; d17 remaining clause (delete the embedded `rust.hw1f` registry block once holder routes stop reading it); d18-d21, d23-d28.

#### #11 Equity LSMC sparse dates; captured-path IRR (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):** (a) `models/src/monte_carlo/pricer/lsmc.rs`, `train_exercise_policy` — ONE skip site, not the two the audit cites: when `regression_x.len() <= basis.num_basis() + 10` the date is skipped and omitted from `ExercisePolicy.coefficients_by_date`, so replay never exercises there. Rows, not base groups, are counted; nothing is reported; `ExercisePolicy` fields are `pub`. The record contradicts the audit's core claim: continue-only on a sparse date is the ACCEPTED behaviour (MC-23 d7, "can reduce value"); what is wrong is the threshold, the counting unit, the silence and the lax solver. (b) Captured-path IRR is NOT present: the amount-only `irr(&amounts, None)` was removed from `engine/simulation.rs` and `paths.rs` by commit `d5803f2ad8`. Residue: `SimulatedPath::extract_cashflow_amounts` in `models/src/monte_carlo/paths.rs`, called only by its own unit test.
- **Record remedy:**
  1. For `p` fitted columns count distinct training base groups with at least one strictly in-the-money leg (an antithetic pair is one group). Fewer than `max(2,p)`, including zero, freezes continue-only at that date. Remove the `+ 10`. No basis downgrade, no realized-future fallback. MC-23 d7
  2. Record every exercise date in the policy as a continue-only or a fitted rule with eligible group and leg counts; a continue-only node always continues in rollback and replay. MC-23 d7 (report clause), d8 (continue-only clause)
  3. Make `ExercisePolicy` Rust-owned and immutable: private fields, ordered per-date rules, read accessors only. MC-23 d10 (immutability and per-date-rule clauses)
  4. An admitted fit must be a checked full-rank fit; a malformed or failed admitted fit errors with its date and counts. MC-23 d7 (fit clause)
  5. One strict solver in `pricer/lsq.rs` replaces the truncating one (no second solver): validate shapes and finite values with `n >= p >= 1`, returning errors instead of panicking; intercept stays one, other columns and the response are divided by their training max-abs, a zero non-intercept column fails; nalgebra SVD with machine-epsilon convergence, an explicit positive iteration limit and explicit `0 < tau < 1`, requiring `sigma_min/sigma_max > tau` (equality fails); solve on every admitted direction; remove the `sigma_max*eps*max(n,p)` cutoff and truncated pseudoinverse; intercept-only uses the checked mean; no retry at smaller degree. MC-24 d1 (shape clause), d5, d6
  6. `tau` and the iteration limit are required explicit fit controls on `LsmcConfig`, hence on `LsmcPricer::new` in Rust, Python and WASM; no registry default for them. `LsmcPricer::new` already takes 7 inputs in all three languages, so two more exceed the `too_many_arguments` threshold of 7: the constructor becomes one params struct in Rust and one params object in Python and WASM (`AGENTS.md`; record silent on the shape, open question 9). MC-24 d6, d10 (fit-controls clause)
  7. Delete `extract_cashflow_amounts` and its test assertion; add no IRR/XIRR to generic capture. MC-28 d5 (amount-only clause); CC-017 d12 (captured-path clause)
- **Differs from the old plan:** Old plan fitted an adaptive reduced-degree or intercept-only regression on sparse dates and rewrote capture IRR as time-weighted root-finding. The record freezes continue-only against `max(2,p)` base groups with a strict solver and no degree fallback, and removes capture IRR (already landed).
- **Files:** Rust `models/src/monte_carlo/pricer/{lsmc.rs,lsq.rs,mod.rs}`, `models/src/monte_carlo/paths.rs`, `models/tests/model_audit_regressions.rs`, `models/benches/{mc_hot_paths,mc_scaling}.rs`. Python `finstack-quant-py/src/bindings/models/monte_carlo/pricers.rs`, `finstack_quant/models/monte_carlo/_pricers.pyi` (plus `mod.rs`, `__init__.py`, `__init__.pyi` if step 6 adds a params class); tests `tests/models/{test_analyst_monte_carlo,test_credit_review_regressions}.py`, `tests/{test_models_host_bridges,test_models_wasm_parity}.py`, `tests/data/models_parity_golden.json` (key `monte_carlo.lsmc`); `benchmarks/bench_bindings.py`; notebooks `examples/notebooks/07_advanced_quant/{monte_carlo_simulation.ipynb,monte_carlo/exotic_payoffs_and_pricers.ipynb}`, `02_pricing/instruments/futures_and_listed_options.ipynb`. WASM `src/api/models/monte_carlo.rs`, `exports/models/monteCarlo.js` (+ `index.d.ts`), `tests/facade/{models_parity,models_handles}.test.mjs`. Parity `parity_contract.toml` (`LsmcPricer` notes). Schema: none (`basis_kind.schema.json` untouched). Other callers of the shared solver, which must each pass `tau` and a limit and lose truncation: `models/src/rates/hw1f/lsmc.rs` (#10), `models/src/monte_carlo/pricer/polynomial.rs` (bond policy, `valuations/fixed-income/src/bond/pricing/tree/lsmc/`), `valuations/rates/src/swaption/pricing/{monte_carlo_lsmc.rs,lmm_bermudan.rs,tests/numeraire.rs}`, `valuations/rates/tests/performance/lmm_consumer.rs` (open question 3).
- **Tests:** date with `max(2,p) - 1` groups is continue-only and reported; with `max(2,p)` it fits or errors on rank; an antithetic pair counts once; solver near-threshold ratio, zero column, all-zero response, shape mismatch. Pinned old behaviour to re-derive: `test_lsmc_few_itm_paths`, `test_lsmc_insufficient_itm_paths_preserves_continuation` (threshold `p + 10`), `test_lsmc_high_degree_polynomial` (raw powers may now fail the rank gate and must be asserted as an error or moved to a conditioned basis), `test_solve_least_squares_singular` (expects finite coefficients after truncation), `gbm_american_prices_are_bit_stable` (column scaling changes bits), host LSMC values in the Python and WASM tests above.
- **Deferred:** MC-23 d1-d3 (contract scope, stored-knot exercise list, node-time discounting); d4 (budgets, removal of registry pricing defaults); d5 (disjoint Philox ID ranges replacing `pricing_seed`); d9 (origin tie rule, a separate site); d10 identity clauses; d11; d12, d13 (remove in-sample `price`, intrinsic floor, `*_unbiased` names; single two-pass `price_american`); d14. Per-date rules live on the policy and `tau` survives as a fit control, so no fit or policy code added here is deleted by them; the exception is the `LsmcPricer` params object of step 6, which d4, d13 and MC-24 d2/d10 re-shape again (open question 9). MC-24 d2, d10 (`BasisKind` removal, strike-centered powers only: until then raw-power and Laguerre fits that are not full rank simply error); d3, d4 (bond owner); d7, d8; d9; d11. MC-28 d1-d4, d6, d8-d12 and the rest of d5 (capture count, storage, event loop, typed cashflows, raw dispatch) except where cited in #18.

#### #18 RQMC replicate design, core Sobol contract, process and scheme validation (Major)

- **Defect (verified in source):**
  (a) Core Sobol, `core/src/math/random/sobol.rs` — NOT as audited: only `try_new` exists and it already rejects dimension 0 and >40, so no zero-filled tail can be built. Real hazards: buffer length checked only by `debug_assert!` (a partial last point is truncated in release), zero seed as "unscrambled" sentinel, modulo wrap in `value_at`/`skip`, implicit `reset` at the period.
  (b) Sobol pricing, `models/src/monte_carlo/pricer/path_dependent.rs` (directory is `pricer/`; there is no `price_rqmc`, the route is `price_with_sobol` + `for_each_sobol_path`): `min(16, num_paths)` replicates of `base + (replicate < remainder)` points, unequal and not powers of two; pooled point mean reported with a Student-t SE/CI over unweighted replicate means and a point-level `std_dev`; seeds remapped by `.max(1)`; `price_asian_pair` accepts Sobol.
  (c) `process/piecewise_gbm.rs`: four `pub` unvalidated vectors; last-interval extrapolation; `step` freezes the interval at `t` for the whole `dt`; bootstrap skips nonincreasing checkpoints and rejects `dt <= 1e-6`. In the two production callers (`valuations/equity/src/{autocallable,cliquet_option}/pricer.rs`) every checkpoint is also a required grid time, so crossing is latent — but today `TimeGrid::uniform_with_required_times` (`core/src/math/time_grid.rs`) dedups knots within `1e-10` and can keep a uniform knot in place of a required time (removed by Slice 0 0a, which makes the builder an exact union), both callers dedup checkpoints at `1e-10`, and the cliquet caller decides whether to append the terminal checkpoint with a third tolerance (`last < t - 1e-6`; inert today because its horizon is the last reset's own time).
  (d) `process/gbm.rs`: `drift()` ignores `drift_schedule`; `simulate.rs` offers `euler`, `log_euler`, `milstein` for `gbm`/`multi_gbm`. Reachable only from Rust (raw `ProcessSpec::Gbm` carries no schedule).
  (e) `process/gbm_dividends.rs` + `discretization/exact_gbm_dividends.rs`: constructor admits `t = 0` while the `(t, t+dt]` selector never applies it (the raw `simulate.rs` route already rejects `t <= 0` in its own duplicate check); 4-epsilon boundary tolerance and `t + dt` reconstruction; in-step splitting with a `<= 1e-10` skip; `num_factors()` is `dividends.len() + 1`.
  (f) `process/multi_ou.rs`: `validate()` does not check the sign of `kappas`.
  (g) `discretization/rough_heston.rs`: `new(times, hurst: f64)` precomputes tables; `step` ignores `t`, uses runtime `dt`, never compares its Hurst with the process, keeps the step in `work[2n]` as `f64`, NaN-fills past `n`; the engine checks step count only (`expected_num_steps`); `simulate.rs` and `valuations/equity/src/equity_option/rough_heston_mc_pricer.rs` each regenerate the kernel times as `T * i / n` beside the `TimeGrid` they simulate on, and `simulate.rs` alone holds the 8,000 cap (the scheme only warns above 200 steps).
- **Record remedy:**
  Core Sobol (a)
  1. `SobolRng::new(dimension, scramble_seed: Option<u64>) -> Result<Self>` replaces `try_new`; `None` unscrambled, `Some(0)` runs the hash permutation; remove the zero sentinel. CN-18 d2
  2. `fill_u01`/`fill_std_normals` return `Result<()>`; length an exact multiple of dimension in every build; preflight shape, point count and remaining period before any write; empty fills succeed; commit state only after a whole fill succeeds. CN-18 d10, d11
  3. Publish `SOBOL_PERIOD` (`u64`, 2^32) beside `MAX_SOBOL_DIMENSION`; remove modulo advancement, implicit reset and wrap; over-consumption errors; `skip(n) -> Result<()>` checks `n <= P - index` before mutating. CN-18 d8, d9, d12
  4. Rename private "Owen" names/docs to "seeded prefix hash digit permutation" and drop Owen-variance claims (formulas unchanged). CN-18 d4-5 (naming and claims clauses)
  5. Delete the `RandomStream for SobolRng` adapter (`models/src/monte_carlo/rng/sobol.rs`). No implementor then returns `is_quasi_random() == true` (`SobolPathStream` keeps the default `false`), so delete that trait method, the rejection in `engine/pricing.rs` and the two engine tests that build a `SobolRng` as stale references; the generic engine then rejects QMC by construction, since no quasi-random `RandomStream` exists (records silent on the trait method). Slice 0 step 0c-2 (its step 13) only re-signatures this adapter's two forwarding bodies; this step has no dependency on steps 1-4 and can move into 0c-2 unchanged (open question 8). CN-18 d13; MC-02 d8
  Sobol pricing (b)
  6. One explicit sampling mode on `PathDependentPricerConfig` (pseudorandom, or Sobol with root seed, `R`, `M`, chronological-or-bridge) replaces `use_sobol`, `use_brownian_bridge`, `with_sobol`'s implicit bridge default and the registry defaults; reject parallel, antithetic, a construction choice on the pseudorandom route and a conflicting total-count override before drawing. MC-03 d1, d8
